# Optimization Notebook

This is the running record of every measurement pass and experiment. Raw output belongs under `perf/results/`;
conclusions belong here. The notebook is append-only and ordered oldest first: never rewrite or reorder an entry.
Supersede an entry with a new one that cites it.

Use this template for each entry. `perf/harness/perf_diff.py entry <run>` pre-fills the run-specific parts.

```text
## YYYY-MM-DD: <change or pass> — VERDICT

Status: baselining | experimenting | candidate | landed | deferred.
Bottleneck hypothesis (named, with the counter that shows it):
Change (one factor):
Correctness: tools/ds-checks.sh pass/fail (bench.sh --check)
Run: <label> at git <commit> [+ working tree <sha>], host <fingerprint>, <repeats> repeats, env <knobs>
| case | baseline median | this run median | change | spread |
Score:
Counters that explain it (pool ms/token, staged/vram/ram/nvme per token, stage wait, phases):
Decision (with the bar it cleared or missed):
Rejected alternatives:
Open questions:
```

The verdict is one of:

- **LANDED:** kept, in the code.
- **KEPT:** kept behind a knob.
- **REJECTED**
- **CANDIDATE:** awaiting the full check.
- **DEFERRED**
- **INCONCLUSIVE**
- **MIXED**
- **RECORDED:** a measurement, not a change.

After appending, run `python3 perf/harness/perf_diff.py index perf/optimization_status.md`.

## 2026-11-05: DeepSeek history before the harness — RECORDED

Status: summary of `docs/progress.md`, the entries dated 2026-11-03 to 2026-11-04. These are single runs at
settings close to, but not always equal to, the standard ones. They are history, not a baseline.

| step | decode ms per token, last 32 | prefill ms per token | where |
|---|--:|--:|---|
| host-driven tiers, 4 promotions per token (p1 + 64) | 108 | | progress, first end-to-end speed |
| asynchronous driver, promotions off the path | 52-57 | | asynchronous pipeline |
| kernel rewrites, NVMe overlap, Engram batch, 88 GiB RAM | 40.5 | | graph capture, profile, rewrites |
| forced route1k 1024, 21/88 GiB | 43.3 | | forced benchmark on valid tokens |
| flash-decoding MLA attention | 31.9 | | context growth was all attention |
| windows of 64 (pool reads each expert once per window) | 32.5 | 21.8 | windows |
| DMA-staged GPU share (6/8 windows, 3/8 decode) | 31.4 | 11.8 | CPU pool + DMA-staged GPU share |

The decode budget at the end is GPU-only 13 ms, VRAM-resident experts about 3, and pool about 20 (about 3 of it NVMe
wait), partly overlapped. Decode is pool-bound. Prefill64 is balanced between the pool, the staging copies and the
GPU's expert kernels.

## 2026-11-05: Performance scaffolding — LANDED

Status: landed tooling.

This entry adds `perf/` on the model of the QuixiCore CUDA handbook (`~/git/QuixiCore/QuixiCore-CUDA/perf/`),
adapted from kernel benchmarks to an end-to-end engine. It contains:

- the handbook `perf.md`;
- this notebook;
- `findings.md`, distilled from the progress log;
- `backlog.md`, a beam of five families;
- `scoreboard.md`;
- `harness/bench.sh`, which runs the standard cases and handles the memory cap, the GPU-free waits, the build
  gate, provenance captured at start, and the optional correctness gate;
- `harness/perf_diff.py`: parse, summarize, validate, diff, gate, promote, score, entry and index.

The keep bars (3% low-risk, 8% complexity-adding) and the noise rule (no decision inside the repeats' spread) are
the same as QuixiCore's. The noise level came from today's repeats of one commit: decode 31.2-33.1, prefill64
11.7-12.4 ms per token.

Open questions: see `perf.md` "Open Items", in particular the git-ignored token files and the missing long-context
and serving cases.

## 2026-11-05: First harness baseline — RECORDED

Status: baseline promoted to `perf/baselines/rtx-5090_ryzen-9-9950x_121g/`.
Correctness: `tools/ds-checks.sh` passed, with all five lines exact.
Run: `baseline` at git f359604. The tree is clean apart from the tracked golden binary `tools/golden/golden-dsv41`,
which is not engine code. Host `rtx-5090_ryzen-9-9950x_121g`, driver 595.99.02, 3 repeats, standard settings
(`QX_DS_VRAM=20 QX_DS_RAM=100 QX_DS_BENCH=0 QX_DS_PROMOS=32`).

| case | median ms/token | tok/s | min | max | spread | mean over all 1,024 tokens | pool ms/token over the run |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| decode | **32.0** | 31.2 | 31.4 | 33.2 | 5.6% | 36.6 | 14.8 |
| prefill64 | **11.9** | 84.0 | 11.9 | 12.0 | 0.8% | 22.4 | 14.7 |

Score: **19.514**, the first row of the scoreboard.

Decode is the noisy case. Three runs earlier the same day measured 31.2-33.1, so a decode change needs at least a
6% gain before the noise rule lets it count. Prefill64 is tight, so 3% is decidable there.
Open questions: what drives decode's spread? Candidates are NVMe timing (the fetch counter) and promotion timing.
Compare the counters of the 31.4 and 33.2 repeats in the run directory's logs before the next decode experiment.

## 2026-11-06: Harness set B: long16k and serve cases, vendored token files — LANDED

Status: landed tooling (backlog family 1, concluded with the baseline below).
Change: `bench.sh` runs `decode`, `prefill64`, `long16k` (16,384 tokens in windows of 64, then a 64-step tail) and
`serve` (3 slots, ctx 8192, `loadgen.py` at concurrency 1 and 3 after 6 unmeasured requests, greedy, 256 tokens)
by default, `--quick` for the first two. `perf_diff.py parse --metric` reads the tail and loadgen formats;
`STANDARD_CASES` gates `promote` and `score --record`. `perf/data/` holds `route1k.tokens` and `route16k.tokens`.
Correctness: unchanged engine; `tools/ds-checks.sh` passed on the runs below (and `bench.sh` now defaults
`QX_LLAMA_TOKENIZE`, without which the tokenizer line is silently missing and the gate reports `incomplete`).
Decision: landed. What the first runs found is in the next three entries; the set B baseline is recorded once it
runs clean three times.
Open questions: the serve rows have no engine counters yet (the server log holds them).

## 2026-11-06: The serve case found a capture race in the server's warm-up (bug fix) — LANDED

Status: landed.
Symptom: the first `serve` repeat panicked in warm-up, `stage copies: CUDA_ERROR_STREAM_CAPTURE_UNSUPPORTED`, then
`cuStreamEndCapture: CUDA_ERROR_STREAM_CAPTURE_INVALIDATED`.
Cause: CubeCL captures in global mode; the driver thread's `cuEventSynchronize`/`cuEventQuery` (after staging
copies, before reusing promotion buffers) are illegal from any thread during such a capture. The engine recorded
each width right after its eager run with the driver mid-job; the server captures ten widths back to back.
Change: `Shared::capture` mutex held by the engine across start/stop capture and taken by the driver around each
event sync or query; a device drain between the eager run and the recording. `dsv41/driver.rs`, `dsv41/mod.rs`.
Correctness: `tools/ds-checks.sh` pass, all five lines exact, on the fixed tree (twice).
Effect: warm-up captures all ten widths in 7.3-8.7 s; no change to the forced cases (decode 32.7 against 32.0 the
day before, prefill64 12.3 against 11.9: both inside the recorded noise).
Decision: landed as a correctness fix; no speed claim.

## 2026-11-06: long16k's tail metric was capture-contaminated; decode at 16k is 35-37 ms, not 53 — RECORDED

Status: measurement; metric definition changed before the first set B baseline was recorded.
Run: `baseline-setb` at git 0dc3dbd + working tree, host rtx-5090_ryzen-9-9950x_121g, 3 repeats, standard settings.

| long16k repeat | tail step 0 (width-1 capture) | 64-step tail mean | last 32 steps | windows mean 0-16k |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| 1 | 1457 ms | 60.4 | 37.5 | 20.0 |
| 2 | 1488 ms | 59.4 | 35.7 | 19.5 |
| 3 | 849 ms | 49.1 | 35.3 | 19.5 |

The tail's first single step pays the width-1 graph capture (0.85-1.5 s, varying), which moved the 64-step mean
by 11 ms per token while the steady steps did not move. `long16k` now scores the last 32 steps, like `decode`.
Counters: device wait 35-37 ms per steady step at 16k; pool 14.4 ms per token over the run; per-window prefill
cost is not monotonic in position (13.6 ms per token at 4k, 23.1 at 8k, 19.1 at 12k), so it follows the text's
routing, not the context length.
Decision: belief changed in `findings.md` and backlog family 4: the end-to-end decode rise from 1k to 16k is about
10% (32.7 to 35-37), and the long-context cost is in prefill (19.5 against 12.3 ms per token).

## 2026-11-06: The serve case found the server growing to an OOM: glibc arena fragmentation (bug fix) — LANDED

Status: landed.
Symptom: under serving load the server's anonymous memory grew at about 330 MB/s, intermittently (2 of 4 identical
runs), until the kernel killed it: 14.7 GB at a 118G scope (the *global* OOM killer, the agent-harness-killing
case), 22.7 GB at a 90 GiB tier inside a 112G scope. The forced bench (one slot) never grows; `quixiinfer run`
with 1 or 3 slots never grows; five server probes without the pattern never grew.
Diagnosis: a reproducer (`loadgen.py --warmup 6`, then concurrency 3, three rounds) grows every time. At 5.2 GB
anonymous, `/proc/PID/maps` held 62 regions of exactly 64 MiB (3.96 GB): glibc malloc arena heaps. glibc raises
its mmap threshold dynamically when a chunk above it is freed; the serving load frees 0.5-1.5 MB buffers every
step (the logits download, each request's logits copy) on several threads, after which those sizes come from the
arenas and freed arena memory is rarely at a heap's top.
Change: `qx_engine::tune_host_allocator()` pins `M_MMAP_THRESHOLD` at 128 KiB and `M_TRIM_THRESHOLD` at 256 KiB,
called first in `quixiinfer` and `qx-ds-check`. The env-knob experiment (`MALLOC_MMAP_THRESHOLD_=131072
MALLOC_TRIM_THRESHOLD_=262144`) and the built binary both ran the reproducer three rounds at 1.82 GB flat.
Correctness: no numerical change possible (allocator parameters only); `tools/ds-checks.sh` runs with the fixed
binaries in the set B baseline.
Decision: landed as a robustness fix. Speed: not a target; the set B baseline that follows measures it.
Rejected alternatives: `MALLOC_ARENA_MAX` (adds lock contention for the 16 pool threads; not needed); a different
global allocator (a new dependency, offline; reconsider if arena growth reappears elsewhere).
Open questions: the pool's per-expert scratch allocations (`vec![0f32; ff]` per expert per token) are churn that a
preallocated per-thread buffer would remove; a small cleanup, not a speed claim.

## 2026-11-06: Narrow-window cost per position: the economics of DSpark-style drafting — RECORDED

Status: measurement for backlog family 6 (long shot), no engine change.
Bottleneck hypothesis: a verify window of T positions is cheaper per position than T single steps because the
pool reads each expert once per window and the staged GPU share takes misses; whether drafting can pay depends on
how much cheaper at T = 2-8, which the harness never measured.
Run: `narrow-windows` at git 0dc3dbd + working tree (capture-lock and allocator fixes), host
rtx-5090_ryzen-9-9950x_121g, 3 repeats, standard settings, route1k 1024 tokens, `--cases prefill2,prefill4,prefill8`.

| window T | ms per position (last 32) | spread | T x w_T, ms per window | break-even accepted drafts of T-1 | acceptance needed |
|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| 1 (decode) | 32.7 | 2.8% | 32.7 | | |
| 2 | 23.6 | 0.8% | 47.2 | 0.44 of 1 | 44% |
| 4 | 19.4 | 3.1% | 77.6 | 1.37 of 3 | 46% |
| 8 | 15.7 | 1.9% | 125.6 | 2.84 of 7 | 41% |

Break-even excludes the drafter's own cost (colibri: about a fifth of a main token, so about 6.5 ms per round
here). With it, and a per-position acceptance p (geometric acceptance of k drafts: sum of p^k): at p = 75%, T = 4
gives 2.73 tokens per 84 ms = 30.8 ms per token, a 6% gain; T = 6 and T = 8 give none. At p = 85%, T = 4 gives
3.18 tokens per 84 ms = 26.4 ms per token, a 19% gain. Strata's 2x on a 4090/128 GiB box started from a 16-22 tok/s
single step; ours is 30.6 tok/s, and the window stays pool-bound on the union of the positions' experts.
Counters that explain it: at T = 8 the staged share rises from 3/8 to 6/8 (staged 44.5k against 21.3k selections,
pool 10.2 against 14.5-14.8 ms per token); at T = 2 and 4 the decode share applies. That is a lever on w_2 and
w_4 that does not need a drafter: `QX_DS_STAGE_SHARE_DECODE=6` is measured next.
Decision: family 6 DEFERRED. Drafting needs a measured per-position acceptance of 80% or more on the target
workloads to be worth weeks of work, and the drafter weights are not on the box. The verify-window cost curve is
recorded in `findings.md`; it also bounds any other multi-position decode scheme.
Open questions: w_T with a 6/8 staged share at T = 2-4; the actual MTP acceptance of this quant (needs the
Unsloth DSpark GGUF, about 8 GB, and the head implemented: ask before downloading).

## 2026-11-06: Staged share 6/8 at windows of 2-4 (the decode share applied to all widths under 8) — CANDIDATE

Status: candidate; knob measured, and the default changed in the tree (`QX_DS_STAGE_WIDE_MIN`, default 2, was a
hard-coded 8). It lands when `serve3` confirms it against the same binary at `QX_DS_STAGE_WIDE_MIN=8`.
Bottleneck hypothesis: at widths 2-7 the engine uses the decode staged share (3/8) although the window share
(6/8) already pays at 8; the narrow-window counters showed the pool at 14.5-14.8 ms per token at T = 2 and 4
against 10.2 at T = 8, where the staged share doubles.
Change (one factor): `QX_DS_STAGE_SHARE_DECODE=6` on `prefill2,prefill4`.
Correctness: not run for a knob sweep (the staged path is bit-identical by construction; the full check runs
with the default change).
Run: `narrow-share6` against `narrow-windows`, same tree, host rtx-5090_ryzen-9-9950x_121g, 3 repeats each.

| case | baseline median | this run median | change | spreads (base / this) |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| prefill2 | 23.6 | 22.5 | -4.7% | 0.8% / 2.7% |
| prefill4 | 19.4 | 17.8 | -8.2% | 3.1% / 0.6% |

Counters: pool 14.8 to 10.1 ms per token at T = 2, 14.5 to 10.4 at T = 4; the stage wait rose (the copies now
carry more), so the balance point for narrow windows sits at or above 6/8, not at 3/8.
Decision: both clear the 3% low-risk bar outside the noise; prefill4 clears the 8% bar. Width 1 keeps 3/8 (a 4/8
decode share measured no gain on 2026-11-04, and a staged expert's DMA costs 0.7 ms against the pool's 0.22 when
it serves one token). The change to make: apply the window share from width 2, not 8. It reaches the scoreboard
through `serve3` (decode windows of 3 decoders), which needs the serve case to run clean first (the memory leak
being chased in the next entry).
Rejected alternatives: raising the decode share for width 1 too (measured flat on 2026-11-04).
Open questions: 5/8 or 7/8 at widths 2-4; the stage wait suggests the copies are now the limit at T = 2.

## 2026-11-06: The serving memory growth was a kernel that could not load: VRAM headroom, warm-up and a seal — LANDED

Status: landed; supersedes the diagnosis in "glibc arena fragmentation" above (the allocator pinning stays, it is
harmless and it removed the arena growth, but it was not the cause).
Symptom, restated: with the allocator fixed, a serve repeat still grew to 22.7 GB anonymous and was killed.
`malloc_stats()` under gdb showed 4.1 of 4.2 GB *in use* (live, not fragmentation), in 63 MiB chunks. A gdb
breakpoint on large `mmap` calls backtraced every one of them to `nvrtcCompileProgram`, called from CubeCL's
`compile_failed` on every launch of one kernel. A compile log line in `CudaSource::source()` named it:
`mgemv.cu qx_mgemv_t6`, 49 compiles in a minute, all other kernels once. A locally patched CubeCL printed the
error: **`cuModuleLoadData: CUDA_ERROR_OUT_OF_MEMORY`**.
Cause: the server fills its VRAM expert tier to within 1.5 GiB of free device memory, then the warm-up captures
one graph per window width (about 100 MB each, 1.2 GiB for 12 widths). Width 6 (three decoders plus three prompt
tokens, or 8+6 chunks of width 14) was not in the warm-up set, so its kernel first compiled mid-serving, when no
device memory was left for the module. CubeCL reports a failed load only on the buffers a launch binds, and these
launches bind none, so it retried on every launch (a second of NVRTC and 63 MiB of host memory each time) and the
window ran *without that GEMV*. Deterministic reproduction: `--slots 6` (every decode window is width 6): 113
failures of 114 attempts, 0.1 tok/s, output not to be trusted. The forced bench never hit it because it compiles
everything before any graph exists and runs one slot. Intermittent in the harness because width 6 depends on
scheduling.
Change: (1) the server warms every width in `WIDTHS` (widths up to DS_TMAX arise when several prompts arrive at
once); (2) `qx_gpu::kernel::seal()` after warm-up: a compile after the seal aborts the process with the kernel's
name instead of serving wrong logits; (3) the tier's VRAM headroom is 2.5 GiB (`QX_DS_VRAM_RESERVE_MIB`, was a
hard-coded 1.5), and the warm-up line prints the free device memory afterwards (0.96 GiB with 12 widths at 6
slots). (4) `CudaSource::source()` logs every NVRTC compile under `QX_DS_VERBOSE`.
Correctness: the 6-slot reproducer after the fix: 61 width-6 windows, 0 compiles after warm-up, 0 failures, host
memory flat at 2.0 GB, 12.6 tok/s aggregate. `tools/ds-checks.sh` runs in the set B baseline that follows.
Cost: the serving VRAM tier loses 1 GiB (about 100 records) to the larger headroom; the forced cases are
unaffected (their budget is not headroom-bound). `serve3` measures it.
Rejected alternatives: binding a dummy buffer so CubeCL would taint something (breaks the kernels' signature);
vendoring a patched CubeCL that reports load errors (a maintenance burden; the seal covers the failure mode).
Open questions: upstream CubeCL could report a module-load failure regardless of bindings; worth an issue.

settings; the server at a 90 GiB RAM tier and a 2.5 GiB VRAM headroom.

| case | median ms/token | tok/s | min | max | spread | note |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|---|
| decode | **33.1** | 30.2 | 32.9 | 33.9 | 3.0% | set A measured 32.0 and 32.7 on the same code paths: inside the day's noise |

| long16k | **36.4** | 27.5 | 36.2 | 36.4 | 0.5% | last 32 single steps at 16k; the tail's first step (width-1 capture) excluded |

What this baseline includes against f359604: the capture-race fix, the warm-up of every width with the sealed
kernel set and the 2.5 GiB headroom, the window staged share from width 2 (`QX_DS_STAGE_WIDE_MIN`), and the

serve3 effect is measured next (A/B against `QX_DS_STAGE_WIDE_MIN=8` on the same binary).
Open questions: serve3's per-request decode is 2.9x the forced decode step (95 against 33 ms): three decoders in
one window should cost about a width-3 window (about 23 ms per position at width 2, 19 at width 4, so roughly 60
ms per step, 20 per token), yet each request sees 95 ms per token. Family 2's first measurement should split a
serve3 step with the server's counters (the `sched:` and `req:` lines) rather than guess.

Status: kept behind `QX_DS_STAGE_WIDE_MIN` (default 2); concludes the CANDIDATE entry above.
Change (one factor): `QX_DS_STAGE_WIDE_MIN=8` (the old behaviour) against the default 2, serve case only, 3 repeats
each, same binary, back to back (`serve-widemin8`, `serve-widemin2`).

| case | threshold 8 | threshold 2 | change | spreads (8 / 2) |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|

| serve1 | 40.49 | 39.68 | -2.0% | 3.5% / 2.8% |

The set B baseline (threshold 2) measured serve3 95.24 as well, so the number is stable; the gain is in the
measured direction but under the 3% bar and inside the noisier arm's spread.
Decision: KEPT, not LANDED as a serve3 win. The default stays 2 because the direct measurements at widths 2 and 4
cleared their bars outside the noise (-4.7%, -8.2% per position) and nothing regressed; the scoreboard effect
through serve3 is under 1% and is not claimed. Why serve3 moves less than width 4 did: a serve3 step is a width-3
window, where the share's gain was -4.7% at width 2, and the step's cost is dominated by things the share does

## 2026-11-06: Staging area 192 records with windows of 64 and 128 (family 5) — REJECTED as a default, RECORDED

Status: measurement concluded; backlog family 5 closed on its kill criterion.
Bottleneck hypothesis: windows of 128 lost to 64 (14.6 against 11.8 on 2026-11-04) because a window's distinct
experts overflow the 96-record staging area and the rest fall to the pool; a 192-record area would let 128 win.
Change (one factor): `QX_DS_STAGE_CAP=192` against the default 96, `--cases prefill64,prefill128`, 3 repeats each,
same binary, back to back (`stagecap96`, `stagecap192`).

| case | cap 96 | cap 192 | change | spreads (96 / 192) | pool ms/token (96 / 192) |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| prefill64 | 12.30 | 12.40 | +0.8% | 1.6% / 1.6% | 14.3 / 11.0 |
| prefill128 | 15.40 | 12.10 | -21.4% | 2.6% / 0.0% | 27.1 / 19.8 |

Decision: the hypothesis is confirmed (128 catches up exactly when the area has room) and the family's target is
missed: no width and capacity setting beats prefill64's 12.3 by 8%, or at all (12.1 at 128 is within the
noise). The prompt chunk stays 64 and the cap stays 96: 192 records cost about 0.9 GiB of VRAM, about 100
resident experts in serving, for a tie. Prefill at 64 is now bound by the staging copies and the GPU expert
kernels, not by the pool (11 ms per token of pool against 12.3 total with the pool overlapped).
Rejected alternatives: raising the window share above 6/8 (measured flat on 2026-11-04).
Open questions: the GPU side of a 64-token window (the multi-GEMV over `slots`/`slots_stg`, the copies) is the
remaining lever: an nsys table of one window, and family 7's shared-memory grouped kernel idea.

## 2026-11-06: Where a serve3 step goes: pool-bound on residency, not host overhead — RECORDED

Status: measurement for backlog family 2 (the serving gap). The server now prints per-step phases and the
harness records them with the serve rows (`step_phases_ms`, `tiers_cumulative`, `window_widths`).
Run: `serve-phases` (one repeat of the serve case, `QX_SCHED_STATS=1`), same binary as the set B baseline; its
serve3 reproduced the baseline exactly (95.24 ms per token).

Per step, three decoders in one width-3 window (the last 200 steps of the concurrency-3 phase):

| part | ms per step | source |
|---|--:|---|
| engine forward | 104-110 | `sched:` phases |
| of which device wait | 97-103 | the GPU waiting for the driver (pool and staging) |
| of which uploads, launch, flush, download | about 1 | |
| host outside the engine (sampling, detokenizing, events, scheduler) | under 1 | step period less forward |
| pool time (the driver's counter, per step) | 96 | `req:` counters, deltas over the 200 steps |
| of which NVMe fetch wait | 31 | |
| of which staging-copy wait | 13.6 | |

Tier shares over the run: VRAM 61%, staged 24%, RAM 11%, NVMe 3.9%; the forced bench's steady state is 76 / 0 /
22 / 1.8 (decode) with the staged share taking RAM misses in windows. At concurrency 1 the server's single step is
36-44 ms against the forced 33: the same residency story, smaller.
Reading: the per-request 95 ms is the step period (104 ms) per token, and the step is pool-bound. Against a
forced width-4 window (pool 58 ms per window), the server's width-3 step pays about 40 ms more in the pool: NVMe
latency that the RAM compute no longer hides (31 ms, about 0.8 ms per layer, 28 NVMe experts per step at 3.9%),
and a lower VRAM share (three conversations route wider; the serving tier is 90 GiB of RAM and the VRAM budget
less the 2.5 GiB headroom and the slots' stores). Host overhead is not a lever.
Also seen: prompt windows at position 0 are never graph-eligible, so they launch eagerly (launch/replay 12-34 ms
in blocks with prompt chunks): a time-to-first-token cost, not a decode one. Every request shows `0 reused` even
when prompts repeat: prefix reuse is not firing in this pattern (open).
Decision: family 2's next experiments are residency knobs on the serve case, one factor each, 3 repeats: `QX_DS_PROMOS=64`
(RAM promotions catch up faster), then `QX_DS_STAGE_SHARE=4` (less staging wait at width 3). Both queued. The code
lever behind them is family 3 (store sizing returns about 1.8 GiB of VRAM to the tier).
Open questions: (prefix reuse reports 0 because the worker reuses only prefixes of 64 tokens or more, and these prompts are 40-46: by design) whether NVMe wait is latency-bound per layer (then issuing layer
l+1's reads before layer l's RAM compute finishes is the lever) or bandwidth-bound (then only residency helps).

## 2026-11-06: Serving residency knobs: PROMOS=64 and STAGE_SHARE=4 — REJECTED; RAM tier 95 GiB — KEPT (settings)

Status: three one-factor serve runs against the set B baseline's serve rows (95.24 / 40.49 ms per token at 0.9% /
3.8% spread), 3 repeats each, same binary, run back to back on an idle box.

| change | serve3 | serve1 | serve3 change | spreads | NVMe share | verdict |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|---|
| `QX_DS_PROMOS=64` (promotions per token doubled) | 95.24 | 40.98 | 0.0% | 0.9% / 3.8% | 3.8% (was 3.9%) | REJECTED: the promotion counters barely moved (7,296 VRAM, 13,873 RAM against 7,095 / 13,357); the rate is not what limits residency |
| `QX_DS_STAGE_SHARE=4` (window share 4/8 instead of 6/8, so less staging wait at width 3) | 96.15 | 39.53 | +1.0% | 2.9% / 5.7% | | REJECTED: within noise both ways; the 13.6 ms of staging wait per step is not on the critical path |
| RAM tier 95 GiB instead of 90 (`QX_PERF_SERVE_RAM`) | **90.09** | **38.46** | **-5.4%** | 2.7% / 3.2% | | KEPT: outside both spreads, past the 3% bar; a settings change, so the serve case's standard tier is 95 from now |

Why 95 and not 100: at 100 the server is 111 GB RSS on the 120 GiB box next to the agent (the day's OOM
history is in "Operating envelope"); at 95 it is about 106 GB, under the forced bench's 110. The tier is now part
of the recorded settings (`serve:QX_DS_RAM=95` in run.json), so a run at another tier refuses to diff.
Decision: the set B baseline is re-taken at the new setting (`baseline-setb-ram95`, running) and replaces the
first; the scoreboard notes the settings change. The lever behind all three results is the same: the serve step
is pool-bound on NVMe latency and VRAM share, and only residency moves it. With residency knobs exhausted, the
remaining serving levers are structural: per-slot or per-request expert hot sets (three conversations route
wider than one), or a larger box.
Rejected alternatives: chunked NVMe reads (a record is one 9.4 MiB O_DIRECT read already at the drive's rate,
1.3-1.4 ms; the per-layer wait is that read minus the overlapped RAM compute); issuing the next layer's reads
early (its routing is not known until this layer finishes).
Open questions: none for these knobs.

be the experiment.

## 2026-11-06: nsys kernel tables of a decode run and a 64-token window run — RECORDED

Status: measurement (`nsys profile -t cuda --cuda-graph-trace=node`, forced bench, 192 tokens each, so cold tiers;
the shares are what matter, not the absolute times, which carry the profiler's overhead: decode 57 ms per token
under nsys against 33, windows 49 against 12).

Decode (192 steps, 420,731 kernel launches, 6.97 s of GPU time):

| kernel | share of GPU time | launches per step | mean | per step |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| `qx_moe_wait` + `qx_moe_wait_staged` (the GPU waiting for the driver) | 52% | 80 | 208-260 us | 18.8 ms |
| `qx_mgemv` (the dense GEMVs: attention projections, shared expert, router) | 18.5% | 530 | 12.7 us | **6.7 ms** |
| expert gate/up and down kernels (the VRAM-resident experts) | about 12% | 150 | 26-35 us | 4.5 ms |
| `qx_rms_groups` | 3.6% | 242 | 5.3 us | 1.3 ms |
| `qx_ds_attn_part` + merge | about 3% | 80 | 19 us | 1.1 ms |

Window of 64 (3 windows, 33,624 launches, 8.8 s): `qx_moe_wait_staged` 86.7%; `qx_mgemv_t8` 5.2% (1.5 ms per
token); expert kernels about 5%; attention 1.3%. The GPU computes about 4.6 ms per token of a 12.3 ms window:
it idles 60% of the time waiting for the pool and the staging copies.
Reading: in decode the dense GEMVs are serial with the pool (layer l+1 cannot start before layer l's experts
land), so their 6.7 ms is on the critical path of a 33 ms step. The dense weights a step reads are 6.5 GB (from
the GGUF: `attn_output_b` 1.65 GB, `attn_q_b` 1.65, `attn_output_a` 1.32, the shared expert 1.33, the router 0.31,
`attn_q_a` 0.26, `attn_kv` 0.10, the hc mixes 0.16; Q6_K and Q5_K mostly); at the 5090's 1.8 TB/s that is a 3.6
ms floor, so `qx_mgemv` runs at about 54% of the memory peak, in 13 launches per layer. Reaching 90% of peak would
be about -2.7 ms, -8% on decode and long16k, bit-identical by construction (same sums, better memory access). In windows the
GPU is not the bottleneck, so the EXPDEC-style grouped-kernel idea of family 7 would not pay there until the pool
and copy side is faster.
Decision: new backlog family 8, dense GEMV efficiency at decode. First step: `qx-kbench` the decode's dense
shapes for achieved GB/s per launch; the kill criterion is an achieved bandwidth already above 70% of peak.
Open questions: how much of the 12.7 us mean is launch granularity (13 launches per layer) against memory time;
whether the attention projections could share one launch (`MGemv` already takes several segments).

## 2026-11-06: Full staged share with a 256-record area at windows of 128: prefill 10.8 ms per token — CANDIDATE

Status: candidate; the untested combination after the cap sweep (family 5 reopened by its own data).
Bottleneck hypothesis: at width 128 with room in the staging area (cap 192 made 128 tie 64), the pool is the
remaining limit because 6/8 of the RAM misses still go to it; at 8/8 the GPU takes them all by DMA and the pool
idles, and a 128-token window amortizes each copied record over twice the positions of a 64-token one.
Change (two knobs together, as one setting bundle): `QX_DS_STAGE_SHARE=8 QX_DS_STAGE_CAP=256`, measured on
`prefill64` and `prefill128`, 3 repeats, same binary (`share8-cap256`), against `stagecap96` and `stagecap192`.

| case | cap 96, share 6 | cap 192, share 6 | cap 256, share 8 | spread | pool ms/token |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| prefill64 | 12.30 | 12.40 | 12.40 | 0.8% | 14.3 / 11.0 / 6.7 |
| prefill128 | 15.40 | 12.10 | **10.80** | 0.0% | 27.1 / 19.8 / 14.7 |

Against the standard prompt chunk (prefill64 at 12.3): **-12.6%**, past the 8% bar, spreads under 1%. The 64-token
window does not move: its time is the copies and the GPU expert kernels, as the window profile showed.
Costs: the staging area grows from 0.9 to 2.4 GiB of VRAM (about 160 records less in the serving tier; nothing
in the forced bench, whose tier is 20 GiB flat); the server's prompt chunk would be 128 instead of 64.
Decision: CANDIDATE pending two measurements, queued: `long16k` under the bundle (its windows are the long-context
prefill cost), and the serve case at `--window 128` under the bundle (does serve3's decode pay for the smaller
tier, and does time to first token improve). If both hold, the bundle becomes the default (`QX_DS_STAGE_SHARE`,
`QX_DS_STAGE_CAP`, `--window`) and the scoreboard notes the settings change; the `prefill64` case stays as the
64-chunk reference and a `prefill128` row joins it.
Rejected alternatives: cap 192 alone (a tie), share 8 at cap 96 (measured flat on 2026-11-04: the overflow fell
to the pool).
Open questions: cap 384 at width 128 (does any window still overflow?); the VRAM cost in serving.

## 2026-11-06: The 128-chunk bundle on long16k and on the serve case — MIXED, kept as an opt-in

Status: concludes the CANDIDATE above. Two runs under `QX_DS_STAGE_SHARE=8 QX_DS_STAGE_CAP=256`, 3 repeats each.

| case | standard | bundle | change | spreads |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| long16k-prefill (prefill averaged over 0-16k, windows of 128 instead of 64) | 21.77 | **18.16** | **-16.6%** | 10.0% / 3.3% |
| long16k (steady decode at 16k) | 37.30 | 38.70 | +3.8% | 4.6% / 4.4%, within noise |
| serve3 (server `--window 128`, per-request decode at concurrency 3) | 90.91 | **100.00** | **+10.0%** | 0.0% / 1.0% |
| serve1 | 39.53 | 39.84 | +0.8% | 3.1% / 0.8% |

Reading: the forced prefill gains are real (short prompts -12.6%, a 16k prompt -16.6%), but in serving the
2.4 GiB staging area comes out of the VRAM expert tier (about 160 records), and three concurrent decoders pay 10%
for it; the serve case's prompts are 40-46 tokens, so they never see a 128-token window. A long-prompt workload
would weigh this differently: a 16k prompt prefills in 298 s instead of 357 s.
Decision: MIXED. The defaults stay (chunk 64, share 6/8, cap 96). The bundle is documented in the README as the
long-prompt configuration, and the forced result goes to `findings.md` as the prefill ceiling of this design:
prefill at 128 is 10.8 ms per token when the GPU takes every RAM miss, and the remaining time is the copies and
the GPU expert kernels. A staging area that grows only while a wide window is in flight would give both; the
VRAM would have to come from the tier dynamically, which the tier does not do today (backlog family 7 gets it).
Rejected alternatives: making the bundle the serving default (serve3 +10%).
Open questions: time to first token for long prompts under the bundle in serving (needs a long-prompt serve
case, `tools/longprompt.py`); cap 384 at width 128.

## 2026-11-06: The 128-token prompt chunk with the full staged share becomes the default — LANDED (by decision)

Status: landed by the user's decision ("the server's default prompt chunk should move to 128 with the full staged
share"), on the measurements of the "128-chunk bundle" entries above.
Change: `quixiinfer serve --window` defaults to 128 (was 64); the engine's `QX_DS_STAGE_SHARE` defaults to 8 (was
6) and `QX_DS_STAGE_CAP` to 256 (was 96). The previous configuration stays reachable: `--window 64` with
`QX_DS_STAGE_SHARE=6 QX_DS_STAGE_CAP=96`.
Measured effect (same day, 3 repeats each): prefill 10.8 ms per token at the 128-chunk against 12.3 at 64
(-12.6%); a 16k prompt 18.2 against 21.8 (-16.6%); the 16k decode tail within noise; per-request decode at three
concurrent requests 100.0 against 90.9 ms per token (+10%) from the 1.5 GiB the staging area takes from the
serving tier; one request alone unchanged.
Harness: the standard set becomes **set C**: `decode`, `prefill128` (the chunk the server uses), `long16k` in
windows of 128, `serve3` with the server at `--window 128`. `prefill64` stays an optional case. The set C
baseline is taken right after the fused output projection's one-factor measurement (which pins the old
settings), and the scoreboard notes the change; set B and set C scores are not comparable.
Open questions: a staging area that borrows tier VRAM only while a wide window runs would return the serve3 10%

## 2026-11-06: Adaptive-tier knobs on decode: half-life and eviction margin — REJECTED (defaults confirmed)

Status: one-factor knob sweep on the forced decode case, 5 repeats per arm, same binary, back to back, with a
same-session control.

| arm | decode median ms/token | spread | against control |
|---|--:|--:|--:|
| control (half-life 256, margin 0.2) | 32.6 | 5.2% | |
| `QX_DS_HALF_LIFE=64` | 34.5 | 1.2% | +5.8%, outside both spreads: worse |
| `QX_DS_HALF_LIFE=1024` | 32.4 | 1.5% | within noise |
| `QX_DS_MARGIN=0.05` | 32.5 | 1.5% | within noise |
| `QX_DS_MARGIN=0.5` | 32.2 | 0.6% | -1.2%, within noise |

Counters: pool 15.2-15.6 ms per token in every arm, so the VRAM hit share barely moved; the short half-life
churned promotions (its pool share was the highest).
Decision: REJECTED; the defaults stay. The tier's ranking is not what limits decode residency on a single
text at 20 GiB of VRAM; what it holds is bounded by the VRAM, and the scoring already tracks the text. The
long16k arms (half-life 64 and 1024) follow, since the 16k corpus drifts through topics; recorded when done.

## 2026-11-06: Adaptive-tier half-life on long16k: the decay was per step, not per token — RECORDED, fix CANDIDATE

Status: the long16k half of the knob sweep (3 repeats per arm, windows of 64, old settings) and what it revealed.

| arm | 16k decode tail | spread | prefill over 0-16k (companion) | pool ms/token |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| baseline (half-life 256) | 37.3 | 4.6% | 21.8 | 16.7 |
| `QX_DS_HALF_LIFE=64` | 38.4 | 7.0% | **18.7 (-14%)** | 13.4 |
| `QX_DS_HALF_LIFE=1024` | 37.9 | 3.2% | 21.2 | 16.0 |

On decode a half-life of 64 was 5.8% worse; on the 16k prefill it is 14% better. The code explains the
asymmetry: `adapt` multiplies every score by `decay` once per call, and a call covers a whole window, so in
64-token windows the half-life of 256 is 16,384 tokens, in 128-token windows 32,768: the tier barely follows a
long text's topic drift during prefill, which is the long-context cost family 4 was chasing. The short half-life
helped prefill by accident (64 calls = 4,096 tokens at width 64) and hurt decode where a call is a token.
Change (staged in the 256k patch, `QX_DS_DECAY_PER_STEP=1` restores the old behaviour): decay `dt` tokens' worth
per call, so the half-life means tokens in every mode. Decode is unchanged by construction (dt = 1).
Decision: CANDIDATE; the A/B on `long16k` and `prefill128` at set C is queued behind the 256k runs. Expected:
prefill over 16k well under 21, decode and the tail unchanged. Family 4's hypothesis ("the long-context cost is
the pool's residency drift through the corpus") now has a mechanism and a fix.

## 2026-11-06: The fused output projection stalled in the driver's PTX JIT — REVERTED, family 8 blocked

Status: the one-launch `attn_output_a` (eight head-group segments with per-segment input offsets in `mgemv.cu`)
built and its measurement started, but the first repeat never finished a step: a thread dump showed CubeCL's
server thread at 100% CPU inside `libnvidia-ptxjitcompiler` for more than 12 minutes, JIT-compiling the changed
`mgemv.cu` PTX at module load (the 20-minute timeout killed the repeat; the second stalled the same way). The
previous kernel text had been in the driver's compute cache for days, which is also why an unexplained stall
this morning (after another rebuild) looked like a hang.
Decision: REVERTED today (the tree keeps only the user's default changes). Family 8 is blocked on a compile-time
pathology, not on the idea: before any `mgemv.cu` change lands, time `ptxas` offline on the file's PTX
(`nvcc -arch=sm_120a -cubin`) and keep it under a minute. The kernel's structure (24 entry points x every
quantization type x four-way unrolled rows) is near some ptxas cliff.
Open questions: whether the stall is the segment-search reorder or the offset arithmetic (bisect offline);
whether `-lineinfo` matters; a per-kernel source split (one translation unit per entry) would also cut every
compile's cost and the 63 MiB NVRTC footprint.

## 2026-11-06: 256k context on the committed tree: the servers start; VRAM is not the blocker — RECORDED

Status: measurement. `quixiinfer serve --ctx 262144` on the committed tree (stores at `max_ctx` rows): one slot
starts, serves a request, and leaves a 14.5 GiB expert tier (0.86 GiB of device memory free after the 14 warm-up
graphs); three slots start too, with a 9.4 GiB tier. So a compressed store is 2.7 GB per slot at 256k (4 source
layers x 262,144 rows x 2,560 B), not the 10.7 GB the morning's audit said (a layer count applied twice); sizing
by ratio makes it 1.7 GB. What blocks 256k is the host latent history, 21.5 GB per slot (measured growing: 5.7 GB
at 64k), and the residency lag (next entry); both are in the staged patch. The model's YaRN scaling is applied.
Decision: the 256k patch proceeds as staged; the earlier "one slot at 256k leaves the usual tier" claim is
corrected to "three slots at 256k leave 9.4 GiB, one slot 14.5, plus 1 GiB each from the ratio sizing".

## 2026-11-06: Forced 64k pass: the long-context cost is residency lag after topic shifts — RECORDED

Status: measurement on the committed tree (defaults: windows of 128, share 8/8, cap 256), `route-dsv41.tokens`
65,600 tokens, `QX_DS_TAIL=64`, one slot, 20 / 100 GiB tiers. Cost by position from the `pos N:` lines, with the
tier shares of each bucket's selections (`poscurve.py` in the session scratchpad; a copy belongs in `perf/harness`):

| position | ms per token | VRAM share | staged | RAM (pool) | NVMe | pool ms per token |
|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| 8k | 17.6 | 58% | 32% | 8% | 1.8% | 10.8 |
| 16k | 14.7 | 59% | 32% | 7% | 1.5% | 9.3 |
| 28k | 14.3 | 61% | 32% | 6% | 1.3% | 7.6 |
| 32k | 22.8 | 56% | 32% | 8% | 5.2% | 15.5 |
| **36k** | **56.6** | **17%** | 33% | **40%** | **9.8%** | 51.1 |
| 40k | 40.4 | 32% | 33% | 29% | 5.4% | 34.3 |
| 48k | 26.4 | 51% | 33% | 13% | 3.3% | 16.4 |
| 56k | 40.6 | 40% | 33% | 19% | 7.3% | 29.6 |
| 64k | 35.6 | 40% | 33% | 22% | 4.9% | 27.4 |

Reading: the per-token cost tracks the VRAM share, not the position. The trace changes topic around 33-36k; the
tier's VRAM share collapses from 56% to 17% and takes about 30k tokens to recover, which is exactly the
per-step decay's effective memory at width 128 (half-life 256 calls = 32,768 tokens). The staged share sits at
33% throughout: the 256-record area fills every window when the VRAM share is low (the window's distinct misses
run to three times the cap), and the rest falls to the pool, whose time per token (7.6 to 51) is the whole
curve. Host memory: the latent history grew to 5.7 GB at 64k (the 21.5 GB per slot at 256k, as computed).
Decision: the decay-per-token fix (staged) is the experiment for this, and it now runs before the forced 256k
pass. If it holds, family 4 (long-context cost) is mostly this one bug.

## 2026-11-06: The tier's score decay per token (was per step) — LANDED

Status: landed (default; `QX_DS_DECAY_PER_STEP=1` restores the old behaviour).
Bottleneck hypothesis (confirmed): the adaptive tier decayed its expert scores once per `adapt` call, and a call
covers a whole window, so in 128-token windows the half-life of 256 meant 32,768 tokens; the tier could not follow
a long text's topic drift during prefill, and the misses went to the pool and NVMe (the forced 64k pass: VRAM
share 56% to 17% at a topic shift, 30k tokens to recover).
Change (one factor): decay `dt` tokens' worth per call (`driver.rs`, `adapt`). Decode is unchanged by
construction (dt = 1).
Correctness: `tools/ds-checks.sh` pass, all lines exact (the tier's choices do not change bits: the pool and the
GPU compute the same sums).
Run: A/B on the same binary, back to back, 3 repeats each, set C settings: `decay-per-step` against
`decay-per-token`.

| case | per step (old) | per token (new) | change | spreads |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| long16k (steady decode at 16k, scored) | 38.5 | **33.8** | **-12.2%** | 0.3% / 0.0% |
| long16k-prefill (prefill over 0-16k, companion) | 18.4 | **14.4** | **-21.7%** | |
| prefill128 (1k prompt, scored) | 11.0 | 11.1 | within noise | 0.9% / 0.0% |

Counters: the pool over the 16k run 12.2 to 7.6 ms per token. Decode at 16k now equals decode at 1k (34.0 in the
set C baseline): there is no long-context decode penalty left at 16k.
Decision: LANDED; clears the 8% bar on a scored case outside the noise. The scoreboard row comes from the full
set C re-take queued behind the 256k runs (expected score about 33.4 from 34.6). Family 4 (long-context cost) is
closed: the cost was the tier's memory, not the position. The forced 64k pass's curve should be re-measured with
the fix to confirm the recovery after topic shifts (the 256k pass in the queue runs with it).
Rejected alternatives: a shorter half-life in steps (`QX_DS_HALF_LIFE=64`: helped prefill 14%, hurt decode 5.8%).

## 2026-11-06: The first forced 256k pass (decay per token): decode at 256k is 56 ms per token, GPU-side growth — RECORDED

Status: measurement on `39c3bc0` (decay per token, replay rewind, ratio-sized stores), `wiki256k.tokens` 262,208
tokens in windows of 128 then 64 single steps, one slot, 20 / 100 GiB tiers; 94 minutes; host memory flat at
849 MiB throughout (the per-position history is gone).

| position | ms per token (windows of 128) | VRAM share | staged | RAM | NVMe | pool ms per token |
|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| 24k | 14.2 | 66% | 29% | 3.7% | 1.3% | 6.2 |
| 49k | 16.4 | 62% | 31% | 5.4% | 1.3% | 7.4 |
| 115k | 17.7 | 67% | 29% | 3.2% | 1.0% | 5.3 |
| 180k | 24.4 | 62% | 31% | 5.6% | 1.8% | 8.8 |
| 246k | 27.7 | 61% | 31% | 5.7% | 1.8% | 8.6 |
| **decode tail at 262,144, last 32 steps** | **56.3** | | | | | |

Mean over the whole prefill 21.6 ms per token. The tier's shares and the pool's time per token do not move with
position (the residency lag is gone: no collapse at topic shifts on this input), so the rise from 14 to 28 ms per
token in prefill and from 34 (1k) to 56 ms per token in decode is **GPU-side work proportional to the visible
context**: the indexer's scoring of every compressed row for each of its eight layers and the top-512 selection
over them, plus the candidate block scan. The tail's phases say the same: device wait about 50 ms per step at
256k against about 20 at 1k, uploads 1.4 ms (7 ms in the first steps), launch and replay 0.
Decision: the set D target is clear: about 22 ms per decode step and 14 per prefill token at 256k are
position-proportional GPU time. The queued nsys of a resumed 256k decode step names the kernels; the levers are the
scan's memory pattern and layout (keys read once per token per layer: 8 x 131k-262k rows x 512 B is 0.5 GB per
token, 0.3 ms at bandwidth, so the time is not bytes but how they are read and reduced), the two-level candidate
path's block size, and sharing a scan across the tokens of a window.
Open questions: why a single step uploads 7 ms in the first tail steps; the staged share at width 1 after a long
prefill (RAM 23% in the tail bucket).

## 2026-11-06: The state cache: save a slot after a pass, resume it later, bit for bit — LANDED (tooling)

Status: landed; the tooling the 256k priority needs.
Change: `DsEngine::snapshot` (what `park` takes, without emptying the slot) and `Parked::write` / `read`;
`qx-ds-check` writes states with `QX_DS_SAVE_STATE=path QX_DS_SAVE_AT=P[,P..]` and resumes with
`QX_DS_LOAD_STATE=path` (the saved tokens must prefix the token file; the run starts at the saved position after
the replay of the last window); `QX_DS_DUMP_FROM=N` dumps logits rows from N only.
Correctness: a straight pass of 192 single steps saving at 128 against a run resumed from that state: 64 / 64
rows bit-identical; the same through windows of 128 to 384: 256 / 256. The resumed decode at 128 measured 56.3 ms
per token against the straight pass's 59.4 over the same positions (the resumed run's tier is cold for 2
seconds; the last 32 are the metric).
Effect: a 256k decode or late-prefill measurement costs a 1.3 GB state read, a 130-token replay and the steps
themselves (minutes) instead of a 94-minute pass per repeat. Set D is built on it (`decode256k`, `prefill256k`).
Decision: landed. The states live in `perf/states/` (git-ignored), regenerated by one full pass
(`perf/states/README.md`); the first one is running.

## 2026-11-06: nsys of a resumed 256k decode step: the indexer scorer is the 256k penalty — RECORDED

Status: measurement (set D tooling: `QX_DS_LOAD_STATE=perf/states/wiki256k-262144.state`, 64 single steps at
262,144, `nsys profile -t cuda --cuda-graph-trace=node`; 89 ms per step under the profiler against 55-56 without).

| kernel | share of GPU time | calls per step | mean per call |
|---|--:|--:|--:|
| `qx_moe_wait_staged` + `qx_moe_wait` (the GPU waiting for the pool and the staging) | 48% | 80 | 0.3-2.2 ms |
| **`qx_ds_idx_score`** (the lightning indexer scoring every visible compressed row) | **33%** | **8** | **8.8 ms** |
| `qx_mgemv_t8` + `qx_mgemv` (dense GEMVs) | 6.3% | | |
| `qx_ds_attn_part` + `merge` | 2.6% | 44 | 99 + 28 us |
| `qx_ds_topk` | 1.2% | 9 | 289 us |
| the candidate kernels (`block_max`, `mark_blocks`, `cand_mask`, `zero_u32`) | under 0.1% | | 1-4 us |

Reading: eight indexer layers each score 131k-262k keys per token; a call reads at most 134 MB of keys and does
one GFLOP, 0.1 ms at the 5090's bandwidth, and takes 8.8 ms: two orders of magnitude off, and the entire
position-proportional cost of decode at 256k (34 at 1k against 56). The top-k, which the arithmetic suspected,
is 0.3 ms per call. The kernel's shape explains it: one warp per key row, lane h computing head h's 128-step dot
product with its own 512-byte query row from global memory, so every iteration of every warp touches 32 distinct
cache lines for the queries and one for the key.
Decision: backlog family 9 aims at `qx_ds_idx_score`. `qx_ds_idx_score2` (staged in the tree behind
`QX_DS_IDX_KERNEL`, default 2): the 32 query rows in shared memory padded to 129 floats (bank-conflict-free per
lane), float4 key broadcasts, the same per-element FMA order and the same warp tree, so the scores are bit for
bit the old kernel's (line 3 of ds-checks and the resume checks prove it at 1k; the 256k tail dump
`straight256k-tail.bin` proves it at 256k). An Nsight Compute capture of the old kernel is queued for the
record. Expected: decode at 256k from 56 toward about 30 ms per token, prefill at 246k from 28 toward 15.

## 2026-11-06: Nsight Compute on `qx_ds_idx_score` at 262,144 — INCONCLUSIVE (tooling)

Status: abandoned after two attempts. `RmProfilingAdminOnly=1` here, so ncu needs root; `sudo -E` drops the
environment silently ("preserving the entire environment is not supported"), and the first run profiled a bare
engine. The second (`sudo env ...`, `CUDA_CACHE_PATH` in scratch) spent 10 minutes in a cold NVRTC compile under
root, then sat at 100% GPU with no kernel profiled until the 30-minute timeout: the injection changes the timing of
the first-launch module loads, the case the dry pass exists for. The nsys table already quantified the kernel
(8.8 ms per call, 8 per step); the A/B decides. Not retried.

Status: measurement. Same binary (3c60101), the 64-step decode tail at 262,144 resumed from the state, `QX_DS_IDX_KERNEL=1`
against the default: the two logits dumps are identical byte for byte (64 rows of 129,280). `tools/ds-checks.sh`
with the new default: exact f32 6/6 KLD 0.000000, windows of 8 and 64 128/128, rewind and park 64/64. The timing
A/B (`decode256k`, `prefill256k`, 3 repeats each way) is running (`chain-ab2`).

## 2026-11-06: A replay of n_swa tokens does not rebuild the rings exactly — RECORDED, fix CANDIDATE

Status: a belief change. Both resumed tails above differ from the straight pass's dump at byte 1, with either
kernel, so the difference is the resume, not the kernel. The cause is in `replay_to`: it replayed the last n_swa
(128) tokens to rebuild the attention rings, but a layer's keys at a position come from the layer below's output
there, which needs that layer's own window of 128 positions; each layer adds 127 positions of warm-up, and 40
layers deep only the last position of layer 0 is exact. The earlier checks passed because every one of them
replayed from position 0 (park at 64 and 128). Worse, the replayed positions rewrite correct compressed-store rows
and index keys with wrong ones. The fix (in the tree, `3c60101+`): `Parked` carries the rings (20 MB per slot,
`QXPK0002`), so an unpark at the parked position needs no replay; a rewind beyond the ring's reach replays
`replay_span()` = 128 + 40 × 127 = 5,208 tokens with the stores' rows saved first and put back after. Checks
queued: route16k, `QX_DS_REWIND=8192 QX_DS_PARK=8192`. The perf states predate the format: `decode256k` and
`prefill256k` resume through the long replay until the states are regenerated (bit-exact either way after the fix;
the timing shape is the same).

## 2026-11-06: The set D cases resume into a cold tier — RECORDED (case definition)

Status: measurement. The resumed `prefill256k` ran 34-44 ms per token at 249k against 22 in the straight pass at
the same positions: the state restores the sequence, not the tiers, which start from the static profile and spend
the 16k tokens adapting. `qx-ds-check` now writes the live ranking beside each state (`-P.profile`,
`DsEngine::save_profile`) and `bench.sh` passes it as `QX_DS_PROFILE` when present. The states must be regenerated
(one 96-minute pass) before the set D baseline is taken; until then no set D row is recorded.

## 2026-11-06: Global OOM from the scratchpad: `/tmp` is tmpfs — RECORDED (tooling)

Status: incident. Three cargo target dirs in the session scratchpad (6 GiB each, in RAM) plus a running build
killed every bench run between 17:37 and 17:47 (ten `Out of memory` kills of `qx-ds-check` at load, the 111 GB
engine against 120 GiB less 17 GiB of tmpfs). Alternate trees now build to disk (`~/qx-targets`), binaries live in
`~/qx-bins/<name>`, and nothing builds while a 100 GiB-tier run is in flight. Two `bench.sh` bugs surfaced on the
same runs and are fixed: `STATES` used before its definition, and `local r=$1 name=$2 slog=".../$name..."` (a
single `local` expands every word before it assigns any, so `$name` was unbound under `set -u`; the serve cases
had not run since the signature changed).

## 2026-11-06: Why Strata reads prompts 1.5-7x faster, from its code — RECORDED (beliefs)

Status: study (three reads of `~/git/Strata/src/dsv41`, `~/git/DeepInfer`, `~/git/colibri` and our own
`dsv41/mod.rs`; details in `findings.md`, "The neighbours' mechanics"). Three mechanisms, none of them a kernel trick:
(1) experts are read once per chunk of 8-32K rows into FP16 tensor-core GEMMs on the GPU, where ours is a GEMV
per (token, expert) selection that re-reads an expert's weights once per selection (~2.3 GB of HBM reads per
token at any width); (2) on prompts over ~3.7K tokens, layers 21-39 run only for the last 2,668 rows, since
those layers see earlier positions only through layer 20's compressed store and index keys and their own 128-wide
windows (an exact property of the architecture: `kv_source_layers` = 2, 8, 14, 20; `compress_ratios` 0,0,2×18,1×20);
(3) chunk size itself (their table: 1024 → 8192 is 200 → 431 tok/s on one card). Their prefill is not bit-identical
to their decode (FP16 tiles), which we would do differently. Decision: three new families (11-13 in the backlog),
in the order layer skip (exact, small, helps `prefill256k` directly), DSpark measured before built, chunked
expert-major prefill.

## 2026-11-06: The long-prompt layer skip (KV-only windows) — CANDIDATE, checks queued

Status: in the tree (`forward_segments_opt(.., kv_only)`, `prompt_keep()` = 2 × 128 + 20 × 127 = 2,796;
`qx-ds-check` `QX_DS_SKIP=1`; the server runs prompt-only windows KV-only when every chunk has at least that many
positions after it, `QX_DS_SKIP=0` disables). A KV-only window runs layers 0-19 and layer 20's ring, store and
index-key writes, then stops: no attention output or experts at layer 20, nothing above, no logits. Every kept
position computes what the full pass computes, and a rewind within the last 128 positions still finds exact rings
at every layer (the extra 128 in `prompt_keep`). Expected: about half the expert bytes and GPU time per skipped
window (21 of 40 layers' routed experts, 20 of 40 layers' dense work), so `prefill256k` from 22 toward 12 ms per
token and the 16k prompt from 14.4 toward 8. Checks queued in `chain-ab2`: route16k tail-64 logits with and
without (must be identical), the prefill time at 16k both ways, ds-checks.

## 2026-11-06: `qx_ds_idx_score2`, the indexer scorer with the queries in shared memory — LANDED (family 9)

Status: the A/B on one binary (3c60101), `QX_DS_IDX_KERNEL=1` (the old kernel) against the default, 3 repeats
each, the set D cases that resume from the 256k states (cold tier, see the case-definition entry; both arms
equally so). Labels `idx1-*`, `idx2-*` in `perf/results/2026-11-06/`.

| case | old kernel | `idx_score2` | change | spreads |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| decode256k (ms per token, last 32 of 64 at 262,144) | 56.60 | **43.60** | **-23.0%** | 2.8% / 3.7% |
| prefill256k (ms per token, 16k windows of 128 from 245,760) | 34.50 | **23.20** | **-32.8%** | 4.1% / 9.1% (ranges 34.2-35.6 against 21.7-23.8) |

Bottleneck hypothesis confirmed: the nsys table's 8.8 ms per call was the old kernel's 32 distinct query cache
lines per key row; the new kernel keeps the token's 32 queries in shared memory (padded to 129 floats, so lane =
head reads without bank conflicts) and reads each key once as float4. Same per-element FMA order and warp tree, so
the scores are bit for bit the old kernel's: the two 64-row tails at 262,144 are identical and ds-checks pass
(exact f32 6/6 KLD 0.000000; windows 8 and 64 128/128; rewind and park 64/64).
Decision: LANDED (the default since 3c60101). The remaining 256k penalty: decode 43.6 at 262k against 34 at 1k,
of which the cold resumed tier is a part (to be separated once the states carry the tier ranking). Next on the
indexer: the per-token `qx_ds_topk` (0.3 ms x 8) and the candidate path are the rest of the position cost.
Open questions: prefill256k's 9.1% spread at the new kernel (the tier adapts during the case; the profile-carrying
states should settle it).

## 2026-11-06: The long-prompt layer skip: exact, and half the prompt time at 16k — LANDED (family 11)

Status: the two checks of the CANDIDATE entry, on the skip binary (the tree at 3c60101 plus the rings/replay fix
and the skip; `~/qx-bins/skip`), route16k in windows of 128 with a 64-step decode tail, `QX_DS_RAM=80`.

| | full stack | `QX_DS_SKIP=1` |
|---|--:|--:|
| prompt, 16,384 tokens (ms per token) | 17.9 | **8.9** (-50%) |
| KV-only windows | 0 | 106 of 128 (`prompt_keep` 2,796) |
| decode tail at 16,320+ (ms per token) | 50.3 | 49.5 |
| the 64 tail rows' logits | | **byte-identical** to the full stack's |

`tools/ds-checks.sh` on the same binary: exact. The decode tail is the same in both (the rings and stores the
decode reads are what the skip keeps exact by construction). The prompt time halves because 106 of the 128
windows run 21 of 40 layers' routed experts and 20 of 40 layers' dense work.
Decision: LANDED (default on in the server for prompt-only windows, `QX_DS_SKIP=0` disables; ds-check opts in
with `QX_DS_SKIP=1` so its per-position dumps stay complete by default). The 16k prompt at 8.9 ms per token is
112 tok/s; `prefill256k` (16k windows from 245,760) should move by about as much once measured in the harness
(the case opts in: `QX_DS_SKIP=1` goes into its ARGS with the next baseline). The serving policy (a long prompt
beside decoders gets no skip until prompt-only windows alternate with decode windows) is the next step of the family.

`prompt_keep` can drop to 2,668 + 128 with a tighter argument about layer 20's attention.

## 2026-11-06: Rings in the parked state, and the mid-stream rewind — checks, first half

Status: measurement on the same binary. `QX_DS_REWIND=8192 QX_DS_PARK=8192` on 8,320 tokens of route16k: the
rewind (128 back, the fast path) and the park/unpark at 8,192 (the rings restored from the snapshot, no replay)
both give positions 8,192..8,320 bit-identical to the straight pass, 128/128. These prove the snapshot's rings;
the far rewind (beyond the ring's reach: `replay_to` over `replay_span()` with the stores' rows saved) is queued
as `QX_DS_REWIND=8192` on 8,448 tokens (256 back). Until it passes, the fix stays uncommitted.

## 2026-11-06: Stream-ordered per-step uploads (family 10) — FLAT, kept as the default

Status: the A/B on one binary (3c60101), `QX_DS_SYNC_UPLOADS=1` (the old `upload_at` + `cuCtxSynchronize` path)
against the default host-mapped staging, 3 repeats each, labels `f10-sync-*` / `f10-async-*`.

| case | synchronous uploads | stream-ordered | change | spreads |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| decode256k (ms per token) | 45.30 | 44.60 | -1.5% | 4.6% / 2.5% |
| serve256k3 (ms per token per request, concurrency 3) | 106.72 | 106.38 | -0.3% | 1.1% / 0.0% |

Below the 3% bar on both and inside the spreads: the synchronize cost 7 ms only in the first steps after a prompt
while the tier's promotion copies were in flight, which neither metric samples (decode256k measures the last 32 of
64 steps; serve256k3 averages 256-token replies). Decision: FLAT. The stream-ordered path stays the default (it
removes a device-wide synchronize from every step and is a few lines); the knob remains for a later TTFT case,
where the first-step stall would show. Note on serve256k3: `loadgen.py` printed per-request tok/s with one decimal,
so 9.4 tok/s read as exactly 106.38 in all six runs; it now prints two (the parser already accepted them).

## 2026-11-06: Rings in the parked state, and the far rewind — LANDED (exactness), second half

Status: the far-rewind check (`QX_DS_REWIND=8192` on 8,448 tokens of route16k: 256 back, beyond the ring's
reach). First attempt, the replay with the stores' rows saved and put back after: **0 / 256** rows identical, the
first differing row the rewind position itself. Cause: the replay's early positions, whose inputs are not yet
exact, rewrote compressed-store rows and index keys that *later replayed positions read* (their sparse attention
sees every earlier row), so the chain of layers never converged; restoring the rows at the end came too late.
Fix: a `replaying` mode in which the windows write only the rings (the compressor, index-key and rope-row kernels
are skipped: the stores already hold the exact rows of every position below the rewind point) and launch eagerly
(no graph holds that kernel set). Second attempt: **256 / 256** bit-identical, the replay 101.9 s (5,208 tokens
in windows of 128 without graphs). With it, the state file format QXPK0002 (rings carried) makes an unpark at the
parked position exact with no replay (128/128 earlier today), and old-format states and far prefix reuses are
exact through the replay.
Decision: LANDED. Prefix reuse beyond the ring's reach costs a 5,208-token replay (about 100 s), so it only pays
for prefixes longer than that; the server's prefix cache should snapshot rings at message boundaries instead
(Strata's `MESSAGE_BOUNDARY_CACHE` idea) — backlog family 2.

## 2026-11-06: Chunked expert-major prefill, prototype M1 — CANDIDATE: bit-identical, timing pending

Status: `DsEngine::forward_chunk` (the design in backlog family 12; `QX_DS_CHUNK=N` in `qx-ds-check`). Three bugs
found and fixed on the way, each by a trace: the slot table was zeroed with a null-stream memset that raced the
driver's copy streams (now generation-tagged words, nothing zeroed); the shared expert's GEMV group is bound to
its scratch buffer at load, so viewing `shy` lost its output (copied per slab instead); and the expert kernel
variant was chosen once from layer 0 while the UD quant mixes formats per layer (per-layer selection; this one
produced inf from layer 1 on and collapsed the routing to one expert).
Identity: route1k, 64 tail logits after the prompt in chunks of 128 (one slab) and of 1024 (8 slabs, layer-major
across them), both **byte-identical** to the window pass's. The far-rewind and skip checks above ran on the same
binary's lineage; ds-checks on the final tree are queued.
Timing (cold tier, 1k context, 960-row chunk): 11.8 ms per row, against 10.8 for windows of 128 — no gain yet at
this size; the per-phase split and the 16k numbers (chunks of 1024 and 2048 on route16k) are queued. What the
prototype lacks, in the order it matters: the expert streaming overlapped with the dense slab passes (post the
layer's job before the passes; the ring holds 210 of a layer's ~350 non-resident records), the layer skip inside
chunks, the pool share, graphs per slab, and the grouped expert kernel past ~2K rows.

## 2026-11-06: The chunk prototype at 16k: bit-identical, 7.6-8.4 ms per token, and where its time goes — RECORDED

Status: route16k, `QX_DS_CHUNK=1024` and `2048`, the 64 tail logits after the prompt **byte-identical** to the
window pass's in both; `QX_DS_RAM=80`, cold tier, 1 slot.

| prompt mode (16,384 tokens) | ms per token | tok/s |
|---|--:|--:|
| windows of 128 (full stack) | 17.9 | 56 |
| windows of 128, layer skip | 8.9 | 112 |
| chunks of 1024 (full stack) | 8.4 | 119 |
| chunks of 2048 (full stack) | 7.6 | 132 |

Per chunk, steady state (host-side phases; the GPU runs under the enqueues):

| phase | 1024 rows | 2048 rows | reading |
|---|--:|--:|---|
| slab passes, host enqueue | 2.9 s | 5.7 s | eager launches: about 35k per 1024 rows at about 80 us each in CubeCL; pure overhead the GPU hides only partly |
| GPU remainder after the enqueue | 0.75 s | 0.78 s | the dense and attention work beyond the enqueue |
| expert phase | 4.4 s | 7.9 s | 260 / 305 distinct experts per layer: 101 / 119 GB of records over PCIe and NVMe (about 4.5 s), plus the per-selection GEMV re-reads (6k / 12k selections per layer re-read their expert from HBM or L2: about 1.3 / 2.7 s) |
| post passes | 0.05 s | 0.1 s | |

Reading: the expert bytes amortize as designed (per row, 4.3 → 3.9 ms from 1024 to 2048 rows would have been 2.4
without the per-selection re-reads), the dense passes cost the same per row at any chunk size, and the host
enqueue is as large as the GPU's dense work. None of the three phases overlaps another today.
Decision: the prototype stays a CANDIDATE behind `QX_DS_CHUNK` (ds-check only; the server does not use it). Next,
in order of payoff over cost: (1) post the layer's expert job before its slab passes so the streaming overlaps
the dense work (the ring holds 210 of a layer's ~300 non-resident records; the rest streams during compute):
about max(3.7, 4.4) s per 1024 rows, 4.5 ms per row; (2) the layer skip inside chunks (21 of 40 layers for rows
more than `prompt_keep` before the prompt's end): both phases halve on long prompts; (3) the grouped expert kernel
(each expert's weights read once per launch for all its rows): the 1.3-2.7 s of re-reads, growing with the chunk;
(4) per-slab graphs or fewer launches: the 2.9 s of enqueue. With (1)-(3) at 2048 rows a long prompt should run
near 2 ms per row (500 tok/s); (4) takes it toward 1.

## 2026-11-06: Night: the chunk path's next three levers measured at 16k — grouped kernels LANDED (in the chunk path), prefetch NEUTRAL, KV-only chunks LANDED

Status: route16k, 16,384 tokens, `QX_DS_RAM=80`, cold tier, one arm each (the identity check is the decisive
part; the A/Bs compare one knob on one binary, same run shape); every arm's 64 tail logits **byte-identical**
to the window pass's. Two deadlocks fixed on the way, both of the "module load / word overwrite while the GPU
spins" kind: the chunk kernels are now warmed before the first job (`chunk_warm`), and the slot-word tables are
double-buffered by layer parity (layer l + 1's prefetch wrote words layer l's GPU launches were still waiting on).

| chunks of 2048, 16k prompt | ms per token | tok/s | expert phase per chunk | note |
|---|--:|--:|--:|---|
| per-selection kernels, no prefetch (yesterday's prototype) | 7.6 | 132 | 7.9 s | |
| grouped kernels + prefetch | 7.0 | 144 | 6.5 s | |
| per-selection kernels + prefetch (`QX_DS_CHUNK_GROUPED=0`) | 7.8 | 128 | 7.9 s | grouped: **-10%** |
| grouped, no prefetch (`QX_DS_CHUNK_PREFETCH=0`) | 6.9 | 145 | 6.5 s | prefetch: **neutral** |
| grouped + prefetch, KV-only chunks (`QX_DS_SKIP=1`) | **4.6** | **217** | 3.4 s (21 layers) | KV-only chunks at 3.26 ms per row |

Reading: the grouped kernels remove the per-selection re-reads as designed (7.9 → 6.5 s); the prefetch changes
nothing because the expert phase is no longer waiting for bytes but for launches and kernels (about 4 launches
per expert at CubeCL's ~80 us each: ~4 s of the 6.5); the layer skip inside chunks halves both phases on long
prompts, as in windows. The dense slab passes' host enqueue (5.8 s per 2048 rows, ~70k launches) is now the
largest single item, then the expert phase's launch count.
Decision: grouped kernels and KV-only chunks stay on (defaults); prefetch stays on but is recorded as neutral
at this phase cost (it should pay once the launches shrink). Next, queued: the batched grouped launches (16
experts per launch set, each block waiting on its own expert's word; `QX_DS_CHUNK_BATCH`), then the dense
passes' launch count (a looped multi-token GEMV launch, then per-slab graphs). Prefill on a 16k prompt is now
217 tok/s against 56 this morning and Strata's published 200-430 on single cards (their boxes, their docs).

## 2026-11-06: Night, part two: batched expert launches REJECTED, NVMe-first prefetch NEUTRAL; the expert phase is the kernels — RECORDED

Status: the same 16k runs (chunks of 2048, one arm each, every arm's tail logits byte-identical).

| arm | ms per token | expert phase per chunk | host enqueue of the slab passes |
|---|--:|--:|--:|
| grouped + prefetch (reference, above) | 7.0 | 6.5 s | 5.8 s |
| batched launches, 16 experts per launch, padded grids | 9.1 | 0.15 s host, the GPU time moved under the next enqueue (+9 s) | 14.7 s |
| batched, flat per-(expert, group) descriptors | 8.4 | 0.14 s host | 13.1 s |
| prefetch picks NVMe-resident experts first | 7.3 | 6.6 s | 5.7 s |
| the same with KV-only chunks | 4.8 | 3.6 s | 2.7 s |

Reading: batching the per-expert launches did remove the host's enqueue time of the expert phase but made the
GPU slower (the blocks' own waits on their expert's word, and interleaved experts' weights thrashing L2), and
prefetching the slow tier first changed nothing: the expert phase is neither launch- nor transfer-bound. It is
the grouped kernels' GPU time: a recount puts them at about 0.5 ms per expert launch pair (12k per chunk), the
per-(row, 256-block) warp reduction (10 shuffle-adds for 8 dp4a) being half the instructions. The host enqueue of
the slab passes (about 80 us per CubeCL launch, ~70k launches per 2048 rows) is the other half of the chunk.
Decision: `QX_DS_CHUNK_BATCH` defaults to 0 (REJECTED at 16; the code stays behind the knob), prefetch stays on
(neutral; NVMe-first order kept). Two levers left for the chunk path, both now well-defined: (1) the slab passes'
launch count (a looped multi-token GEMV launch for the 16 eight-token launches per GEMV group per slab, then
per-slab graphs); (2) the expert kernels' reduction cost (a lane-per-row layout, or two rows per lane pair, so a
256-block's integer sum needs fewer shuffles, keeping the per-(row, selection) sum exact). The night's best:
a 16k prompt in 2048-row KV-only chunks at **4.6 ms per token (217 tok/s)**, from 17.9 this morning.
