## 2026-11-10: The placement swept the server's own threads onto the engine's spinning CPU — FIXED (TTFT at concurrency 3: 1.8 s every run, was 2.8-3.3 s in 4 of 6); found by the breaker

The breaker's finding on the placement diff: `place_threads` pinned every other thread of the process to the
engine's CPU; in the server that includes the main thread and the HTTP runtime's workers (`23 other threads on
[15]`), which then time-share with a thread that spins for the whole request. The setd-620 serve "gains" were this:
per-request ms counts after the first token, and the first token came a second later in 6 of 9 concurrency-3
sessions (0 of 6 on the baseline); wall time and aggregate tok/s were unchanged.
Change (chunk53): `pin_engine_early` records the threads that exist before it (the host process's own);
`place_threads` leaves those unpinned (`QX_DS_HELPERS=all` restores the full sweep). qx-ds-check has no such
threads: its placement is unchanged by construction (`0 host threads left alone`).
Arms, same binaries, clean box, three repeats, (ms per token per request, TTFT median s, aggregate tok/s):

| case | new default | `QX_DS_HELPERS=all` (the old sweep) |
|---|---|---|
| serve3 | 96.15 / 1.73 / 28.75, 96.06 / 1.79 / 28.72, 96.43 / 1.80 / 28.63; again 96.71 / 1.75, 96.81 / 1.80, 96.90 / 1.80 | 94.52 / 2.76 / 28.48, 93.90 / 2.83 / 28.56, 96.06 / 1.77 / 28.65 |
| serve256k3 | 106.84 / 1.89 / 25.96, 106.16 / 1.90 / 26.10, 105.82 / 1.88 / 26.13 | 103.73 / 3.32 / 25.76, 104.82 / 3.32 / 25.55, 106.38 / 1.88 / 26.02 |
| serve1 | 38.79, 38.99, 36.98; 37.02, 36.31, 34.76 | 37.33, 38.37, 37.45 |
| serve256k1 | 37.59, 39.05, 41.24 | 38.94, 37.58, 40.23 |

New default: TTFT 1.73-1.90 s in 9 of 9 concurrency-3 sessions and aggregate tok/s equal or better; the sweep: slow
TTFT in 4 of 6. serve3 per-request is 96.1-96.9, the baseline's 96.81: the placement gives the server's
concurrency-3 decode nothing (it is bytes-bound) and the forced decode cases 6-9%.
Other arms of the chain: prefill256k 5.7 / 5.6 / 5.6 default, 5.6 x3 with `QX_DS_HELPERS=free` (the breaker's
item 6: no difference; the +1.8% of setd-620 was its disturbed round). decode256k with the pre-placement layout
(`QX_DS_MAIN_CPU=none QX_DS_CPUS=0-15`): 44.1 / 43.8 / 44.3 against the default's 41.7-42.1 in nine clean
repeats an hour earlier: the placement, not the driver, is the 2 ms (the breaker's item 4), with the caveat below.
**Open: the slow mode is still here on the clean box.** The default's decode256k right after a server case read
44.5 / 44.2 / 41.8 (pool 1,819 / 1,846 / 1,648 ms, gap 1,487 / 1,460 / 1,169). `kcompactd0` burns 5-100% of a CPU
in bursts while an engine holds the pinned tier (free order-10 blocks at 0; `vm.compaction_proactiveness` = 20),
on CPUs 16-21, the SMT siblings of pool cores 0-5 (`~/qx-chains/kcompactd.log`, sampled every 10 s from 00:48).
Engine reads are already O_DIRECT, THP is madvise-only and unused: the compaction is the kernel's proactive
pass over what the 100 GiB pinned tier leaves free. Not yet shown repeat by repeat; the sampler now runs beside
every chain. The cure (a sysctl) needs sudo: the user's call.

The slow first token, read from the server logs (narrow-s256-n6, session 3 against session 2): in the slow
session the second round's three prompts were admitted into one 96-wide window (`window widths {... 96: 1}`,
`launch/replay 20.10 ms` averaged over 110 steps = 2.2 s in that window) and all three first tokens waited for it
(prompt times 4.43 / 3.58 / 3.85 s); in the normal session the same prompts went through 32/48/64 windows
(launch/replay 5-6 ms per step, prompt times 1.2-2.1 s). So it is which window width the arrivals happen to form
(a timing matter that the faster decode steps shift), and the first real use of that width in the session costs
about 2 s of launch/replay: the scheduler's behaviour, not the share. Backlog: first real use of a wide window
after warm-up should not cost 2 s (see what the dry pass leaves undone for width 96).

## 2026-11-10: Promotion churn and the tier leak (conjecturer E1/E5), and the prefetch at width 3 (E2) — MEASURED, zero builds

`qx-chain-leak`, chunk54 binaries, clean box, three repeats. decode256k (ms; counters cumulative over 64 tokens):

| arm | decode256k | vram / staged / pool / nvme records | fetch ms | VRAM promos |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| control | 44.5, 42.2, 42.0 | 9,413 / 2,034 / 3,394 / 560 | 536 | 1,467 |
| `QX_DS_VRAM_PROMOS=0` | 49.0, 49.2, 49.0 | 5,806 / 3,502 / 5,835 / 257 | 162 | 0 |
| `QX_DS_VRAM_PROMOS=4` | 47.9, 44.1, 44.0 | 7,200 / 2,946 / 4,905 / 349 | 259 | 254 |

The promotions are worth 7 ms (VRAM hit rate 38% -> 61%), and they also cause more than half of the NVMe fetches:
560 -> 257 records and 536 -> 162 ms of fetch wait per 64 tokens with them off (5.8 ms per token of fetch is churn).
The mechanism is the code read of the conjecturer: the initial VRAM residents have no RAM copy; a VRAM victim is
dropped, not written back, so it is NVMe-only; its replacement keeps its RAM place, and `promote_one` excludes
VRAM residents from the RAM victims (`h.table[x] == 0`), so those duplicate copies are never reclaimed.
Presupposition row 15's premise ("inclusive at the VRAM/RAM boundary") is false for the initial residents.
Next (experiment, complexity class, 8% bar on decode256k or serve256k3 unless it proves trivial): `QX_DS_RAM_DEDUP=1`
(written, off by default): RAM promotions evict the RAM copies of VRAM residents first (hottest resident first,
no margin test, never a flex-tail resident: the refill after a chunk reads its copy).
(The first repeat of each decode arm is slow again: 44.5 and 47.9 against 42-44. Three chains now show it on the
first repeat after a different process shape; kcompactd in the sampler log is quiet in those minutes. To check.)

Server, `QX_DS_STAGE_WIDE_MIN=4` (narrow share 3/8, the default of chunk54), serve256k3 / TTFT / tok/s:
promotions off 139.9 / 2.57 / 19.99 (x3 within 0.4%; serve256k1 52.3-53.9): the promotions are worth 30% there.
Prefetch `mix` K=6 one copy: 96.06, 95.69, 95.60 (28.5-28.7 tok/s) against 97.2-97.4 without (-1.5%, below the bar
and smaller than the narrow share's own 4/8 step); three copies: 99.7 x3 (worse). The copies do not land in the
gap for free: E2 is flat. No change.

## 2026-11-10: Exclusive tiers: VRAM victims copied back into the RAM places of duplicates — CANDIDATE default (decode256k -8.8%, serve256k3 -5.8% in the screens; validation pending)

Row 30. Screens of at most 10 minutes each (the user's rule from today), `vm.compaction_proactiveness` 0 from
04:33:49 (the user), clean box, binaries chunk56-58 over 0bc58dc; decode256k three repeats, serve256k one session
at concurrency 3 (`SERVE_LEVELS=3`); counters cumulative over the 64 decode tokens:

| arm | decode256k | NVMe records / fetch ms | RAM promos | serve256k3 (ms / TTFT / tok/s) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| control | 42.0, 41.9, 42.0 | 557 / 534 | 537 | 93.02 / 2.18 / 29.11 |
| `QX_DS_RAM_DEDUP=1` first form: RAM promotions evict the RAM copies of VRAM residents first | 41.1, 41.0, 40.9 | 529 / 488 | 512 | 91.07 / 2.45 / 29.60 |
| + VRAM victims without a RAM copy queued for an NVMe read ahead of the candidates (now mode 1) | 41.3, 41.0, 41.2 | 309 / 470 | 1,634 | 88.34 / 1.96 / 30.79 |
| + victims copied device-to-host from their quarantined slot into a duplicate's RAM place (mode 2) | **38.3, 38.2, 38.3** | 285 / 308 | 636 | **87.57** / 3.16 / 30.10 |

The NVMe write-back halves the NVMe-served records but not the fetch wait (25 promotion reads per token contend
with the demand fetches: 1.5 ms per record against 0.96); the device-to-host copy has no such cost: pool 1,642 ->
1,391 ms, fetch 534 -> 308. prefill256k with mode 1: 5.7 (one run; unchanged). Mechanism of mode 2
(`driver.rs`, the VRAM promotion plan): on evicting victim v with no RAM copy, pick the hottest VRAM resident x
of the class that still has a RAM copy (not being copied, not prefetch-inflight, not in the flex tail), take x's
place out of the store, `cuMemcpyDtoHAsync` v's slot -> that place on the copy stream (the slot is quarantined two
ticks and any refill is queued behind on the same stream; a flex grant synchronizes the stream first), and adopt
the place for v when the copy event fires (`finish_copies`); until then v is not in RAM and no new VRAM
promotions are planned. Residency only: the weights are read-only and the same bytes come back.
The serve session's TTFT 3.16 s is the 96-wide first-use window again (see the entry above), to confirm.
`QX_DS_RAM_DEDUP` defaults to 2 from chunk59 (0 restores the old tiers).

## 2026-11-10: The economics of a verify window at 256k — MEASURED: drafting does not pay here (long shot, backlog family 6)

decode256k's 64 tokens after the 262k state in windows of W of the same stream, chunk60, one run each, ms per
token (harness value, last 32 tokens; then the per-8-token lines): W=1 38.3; W=2 33.5 (36.4, 41.1, 24.6, 28.1);
W=4 33.2 (61.1, 36.8, 41.9, 25.4); W=6 (23.0-51.4; the harness value 71.3 includes a 1.36 s first-use window of
4 at the end); W=8 31.3 (49.7, 26.7). A window's cost per position falls only from 38 to 25-33 ms between width 1
and 8: the host-served expert bytes scale with the positions. A verify round of T positions accepting `a` drafts
costs T x w_T for 1 + a tokens: at T=8 and w=27-31 that is 213-250 ms, so break-even against 38.3 ms needs
a >= 4.6-5.5 accepted of 7 (66-79%); at T=4 (w 25-33) a >= 1.6-2.5 of 3 (55-82%). colibri's guard sits at 60% and
Strata's DSpark measured 26.9 against 27.5 tok/s on this box: consistent. Verdict: REJECTED for this hardware at
256k. Re-open if a window's cost per position at width 4-8 falls under ~12 ms (it is the experts: 0.9 GB of host
bytes per position), e.g. with a much larger VRAM tier or a second card.

Target (goal prompt, "Match Strata"): a cold 16k prompt at Strata's 765 tok/s (21.4 s; rerun today, DeepSeek
sources unchanged by the user's pull of 2026-11-10). Ours, chunk60, `long16k`: 2.89 ms per token at chunk 8192
(346 tok/s), 2.68-2.77 at chunk 4096 (361-373 tok/s). The gap is 2.05x, not the 4.3x of 2026-11-07 (217 then).
Per chunk at 4096 (ms): 13,059 (first, KV-only; cold) / 8,067 / 7,808 (KV-only) / 15,966 (4,032 rows, full
stack). Row-layers: ours ~0.10 ms per row per layer; Strata 21.4 s over 16,384 x 20 + 2,668 x 20 = 0.056.
GPU utilization sampled at 250 ms across the run: 85-99%, 320-440 W: **the chunk path is GPU-bound; the "slab
enqueue" seconds are back-pressure, not host cost** (conjecturer E8; rows 10/21: kernel-latency-bound).
nsys kernel table (exact path; 35.5 s of kernel time in ~46 s of wall):

| kernel(s) | s | launches | avg us |
|---|--:|--:|--:|
| attention: `qx_ds_attn_part` + `_merge` | 7.42 + 2.01 | 13,040 each | 569 / 154 |
| dense: `qx_mgemv_t8z` / `_t8zt` / `_t8zf` | 5.92 / 3.98 / 0.75 | 8,877 / 34,152 / 4,634 | 667 / 117 / 161 |
| expert gate+up (`qx_moe_gateup_mma_*`: iq2_xxs, iq1_s, iq2_s) | 4.69 + 4.20 + 1.36 | 14,042 / 13,007 / 4,718 | 334 / 323 / 288 |
| expert down (`qx_moe_down_mma_*`) | 2.7 | ~30,000 | 82-100 |
| `qx_ds_hc_mixes`, `qx_ds_idx_score2`, `qx_moe_quant_h_sel` | 0.56, 0.54, 0.33 | | |

Thirds: experts 36%, dense 30%, attention 27%. Every one is tens of thousands of launches of 0.1-0.7 ms, each
far too small to fill 170 SMs: 32-token attention slices, 128-row slabs, one expert per launch.
`QX_DS_CHUNK_GEMM=1` (chunk61, off by default): `mgt` sends windows/slabs of 64+ tokens to `MGemv::run_gemm`
(`qx_gemm2` bf16 tiles with f32 accumulation per segment, `qx_gemm_f32` for F32 rows; the head stays exact;
groups with an x stride other than their columns, `wo_a`, and the shared expert's moded products keep the old
path). Result: last chunk 15,966 -> 14,757 ms (-7.6%), KV-only chunks -3%; kernel time 35.5 -> 33.7 s
(`qx_gemm2` 5.02 s in 24,924 launches at 201 us; `qx_gemm_f32` 1.51 s at 532 us for the router). KL of the 63
positions after the prompt against the exact path: mean 0.0077, p99 0.058, top-1 95.2% (60 of 63); within the
bound, and too little speed to be worth it per slab: a 128-token GEMM launch is as latency-bound as the GEMV.
Plan (Strata's layout, `layer_chunk` in `~/git/Strata/src/dsv41/ds4_model.cu:11387`): phase-major layers with
chunk-wide scratch: (1) the dense groups once over all rows of the chunk (one `qx_gemm2` launch per matrix over
4096-8192 rows); (2) attention in slabs of 256-2048 queries per launch; (3) experts batched across the layer in
one gate/up and one down launch (`qx_gemm_grouped2` exists), sequenced by stream events rather than in-kernel
waits; (4) the first chunk's 5 s of cold cost; (5) the full stack only on the rows that need it (Strata: 2,668).

Step 2 (chunk62, not committed; patch kept at `~/qx-chains/chunk-ffn-wide.patch`): with the knob, the slab loop
runs only the FFN norm and `chunk_ffn` runs the router, the shared expert's gate/up, the selections and the

most of the 10.6 s) and the experts (12.9 s). Not verified: that the wide path was taken on every layer (no

## 2026-11-10: Match Strata, experts: each expert's launch set on one of N side streams — CANDIDATE default N=8 (16k prompt 2.77 -> 2.17 ms per token, 361 -> 461 tok/s, bit-identical; prefill256k 5.8 -> 5.1); validation pending

The kernel table put 36% of a 16k prompt's GPU time in the experts: one launch set per expert (wait for the
staged record, gate/up, quantize, down), each ~130 blocks, in series on one stream. Change: `qx_gpu::raw` gets
side streams (`side_begin` / `side_use` / `side_join`: each side stream starts behind the main stream's work so
far; the main stream joins them by events); the chunk path's expert loop sends expert i to side stream i mod N
with that stream's own gate/up and hidden scratch (indexed by the row's place in the expert's list: an eighth
of the chunk's rows per stream; an expert with more rows runs on the main stream with the chunk's scratch); the
progress mark the staging ring waits for is written on the main stream after a join, every N experts, so it
still means "every expert up to this ordinal is done". Same kernels, same inputs.
First attempt (chunk63): N=2 ran (2.38 ms per token); N=4 and N=8 hung in layer 2 of the first chunk, N=4 with
the expert prefetch off in layer 0 (and sat on bench.sh's 2400 s timeout twice: 80 minutes against the user's
10-minute rule; every chain run now carries its own `timeout 200`). With `CUDA_DEVICE_MAX_CONNECTIONS=32` N=4, 8,
12, 16 run: my reading (not proven) is that the context's default 8 work queues are shared by the streams, so a
kernel waiting in place for a staged record can sit in front of the copy that stages it. `Gpu::new` now sets the
variable at start-up when it is unset (chunk64).
16k prompt, 4096-row chunks, one run each (chunks in ms; the 233 GiB download had finished at 07:51):

| N=8 (chunk64) | 11,217 / 6,192 / 5,881 / 12,475 | 2.17 | 4096 / 4096 rows bit-identical |
| N=12 (chunk64) | 11,207 / 6,206 / 5,886 / 12,482 | 2.18 | bit-identical |

## 2026-11-10: Strata's 16k prompt under nsys against ours — MEASURED: Strata spends 6.6 s of GPU kernel time, we spend 38.4 s

The user's request: understand deeply how Strata reaches its prefill rate. Same prompt (`route16k`, 16,384
tokens), same box, both under `nsys profile -t cuda`; Strata as built 2026-11-06 (`--prefill-chunk 8192`), ours
chunk67 (4096-row chunks, expert side streams on). Strata's own `DS4_PROF` stage table crashed (exit 139) on the
chunked prompt, so the table below is nsys only. Strata: 18.1 s wall (905 tok/s this run; 765 earlier today
cold), **6.6 s of kernel time in 115,539 launches**: its GPU idles two thirds of the prompt (expert streaming
from the files and host work bound it). Ours: 36.8 s of chunks, **38.4 s of kernel time in 328,682 launches**
(overlapping streams): GPU-bound. Row-layers are alike (Strata 16,384 x 20 + 2,668 x 20 = 381k; ours 12,288 x 20
+ 4,032 x 40 = 407k), so per row-layer Strata's kernels cost about a sixth of ours, in every class:

| class | Strata | ours | ratio |
|---|---|---|--:|
| attention | `sparse_attn_packed_kernel<8>`: 1.50 s, 1,500 launches of ~1.0 ms (256 queries per launch) | `qx_ds_attn_part` 7.43 s + `_merge` 2.01 s, 13,040 launches each (32 queries per launch) | 6.3x |
| dense products | cuBLAS half-precision tensor-core GEMMs (`nvjet_..._hss_mma_*`): 0.64 s in ~29,000 calls of 17-23 us, + `splitKreduce` 0.09, + weights dequantized to FP16 by `q8_rows_kernel` 0.45 s (26,159 calls), + the router in f32 (`gemv_f32_kernel`) 0.45 s, + `sgemm` for the hyper-connection mixes 0.05 | `qx_mgemv_t8z` 5.97 + `_t8zt` 3.99 + `_t8zf` 0.75 = 10.7 s in 47,663 launches | 6x |
| routed experts | `set_tc_kernel<type>` (FP16 WMMA tiles, weights decoded in-kernel, a batch of experts per launch): 1.94 s in ~1,730 launches of 0.5-1.6 ms, + `wait_seq_kernel` 0.40, + `moe_combine` 0.15 | gate/up 11.1 s + down 4.7 s in ~78,000 launches + quantize 0.47 | 6-8x |
| the rest | `hc_post_inplace` 0.09, `finish` 0.08, `index_score` 0.08, `compress` 0.05 | `hc_mixes` 0.56, `idx_score2` 0.54, `rms` 0.15, `compress` 0.13 | |

What Strata does differently, by weight:
1. **Half precision on tensor cores for every product.** Dense: each matrix is dequantized to FP16 once per
   chunk and multiplied by one cuBLAS `GemmEx` over all the chunk's rows (FP16 x FP16, f32 accumulation): 17 us a
   call. Our exact int8/f32 GEMV reads each weight once per 8 tokens with per-group barriers; our own bf16 tile
   kernel (`qx_gemm2`, decoding the quantized weights inside every tile) took 201 us per 128-token launch.
2. **Experts in batched FP16 tiles.** One gate/up and one down launch for a set of experts (64 x 64 WMMA tiles,
   the weights decoded to FP16 in shared memory), about 1,700 launches for the prompt against our 78,000 of exact
   int8 tiles re-decoded per 64-row pass.
3. **Attention at 256 queries per launch on packed keys** (BF16 window, FP4 compressed rows, each packed row decoded
   once for 8 heads): 3.9 us per query-layer against our 22.6 on exact f32 rows.
4. **The upper 20 layers on 2,668 rows** (`forward_chunks ... keep 2668 skip_rows 13716`); ours runs them on the
   whole last chunk (4,032 rows at chunk 4096).
5. More experts resident: 2,153 in VRAM (22.4 GB) against our 1,780 (17.0 GiB) on this prompt.
Reading: the gap is not launch shape or scheduling, it is arithmetic: Strata's GPU work is ~6x cheaper because it
is FP16 tensor-core GEMM throughout, and it then waits on its expert streaming. With the prefill contract now
KL-bounded, the same arithmetic is open to us, and our tiers already stream experts faster than Strata's
(Strata's GPU idles 11.5 of 18.1 s): an FP16 path could pass it, not merely match it. Order of work: (a) dense:
FP16 dequant per chunk + a real GEMM (cuBLAS through cudarc, or a tile kernel without in-tile decode) chunk-wide;
(b) experts: FP16 WMMA tiles over a batch of experts; (c) attention at 256+ queries per launch; (d) the upper
layers on the kept rows only.
Also from this session: `CUDA_DEVICE_MAX_CONNECTIONS` 8 / 16 / 32 on decode256k: 38.3, 38.5 / 38.9 / 38.3, 38.5:
no cost. `QX_DS_ATTN_STREAMS` (a slab's four attention slices on side streams): 6,194 -> 6,129 ms per KV-only
chunk, -1%: the attention kernels already fill the card; reverted (patch `~/qx-chains/attn-streams.patch`).
Whole-slab pipelining across streams is blocked by `ring_w = n_swa + DS_TMAX` (the next slab's appends would
overwrite cells the previous slab's attention still reads) and the saved 256k states carry that ring.
Validation of the expert streams default (tree 7f7117f, `setd-promo2-085837`): ds-checks pass (two parts),
identity 2 of 2, prefill256k 5.70 -> 5.20, decode256k 38.55, serve256k3 90.74, score 26.299; not yet adjudicated.

## 2026-11-10 (evening): The FP16 prompt path, reviewed: HOLD from the adjudicator, a server crash found, cuBLAS fits no server, the engine's own f16 kernel — corrections to the entry above

**The promotion of `setd-promo5-201304` (tree 25c7614) was not made.** Adjudicator (fresh context): "Verdict:
HOLD. The set D numbers pass the bar and no new promotion run is needed, but two checks are missing and the entry
needs corrections before it can carry a PROMOTE." Its findings, and the breaker's on 4ee0e73..25c7614:
- Every identity session had `chunk steps 0` (40-46 token prompts): they prove the server's decode exact and say
  nothing about the chunk path. With the 13,283-token prompt (`tools/longprompt.py --chars 60000`) **the
  three-slot 256k server on 25c7614 panicked**: `raw: cuModuleLoadData of mgemv.cu: CUDA_ERROR_OUT_OF_MEMORY`
  after cuBLAS took its handle from the 0.45 GiB the server has free after warm-up; the request returned nothing.
  The exact arm: TTFT 31.6 s (420 tok/s), the repeat 2.2 s (prefix reuse).
- In FP16 mode `mgemv.cu` was compiled and loaded after layer 0's experts were posted (the dry-pass rule).
- Corrections to the entry above: the free-memory explanation of the slow decode256k runs is wrong (65 GB free was
  also the state before clean runs; `sar` shows 111 GB free seconds before the slowest run); 5 slow candidate runs
  of 11 in the 19:20-20:02 window (V5A's 42.1 included), 0 of 7 for the promoted binary, 0 of 11 after 20:04; the
  same signature (pool +12%, gap 1190 -> 1500) is on earlier binaries the same day; cause unknown. serve256k1's
  +6% is not noise: the second concurrency-1 request is slower in 8 of 8 sessions since 7f7117f (9.9-10.8 s
  against 9.2-9.5; about twice the NVMe records), unattributed. Identity was 3 sessions on 25c7614 plus one on the
  same binaries. The 599 tok/s (1.67 ms per token) was chunk74 (handle created at load); with the handle created
  at the first chunk a cold 16k prompt reads 1.86 (538 tok/s). The "exact" screen row mixed two runs.
  The larger flex tail (`ChunkBufs::extra`, ~0.8 GB at 8192 rows) leaves 102 VRAM slots empty after the server's
  warm-up instead of 77: NVMe misses +4% and pool time +3.6% in six sessions against five, which fits serve256k3
  at 90.3-91.0 against 89.5 (the breaker; not yet fixed).

kernel, these binaries: 16k prompt at 4096-row chunks, 511 positions: KL mean 0.0089, p99 0.073, max 0.131, top-1
97.5%; the scored configuration (prefill256k, 8192-row chunks from the 245,760 state), 64 tail positions: KL mean

compiled after layer 0, `ffn wide true`, `chunk steps 2` (logs in the run directory). Against the exact kernels

against 31.6 s, one session each) is inside that chunk's noise.
**A consequence for outputs (the breaker's item 10):** the server takes the chunk path only while no slot is
decoding (`worker_ds.rs:300`); a long prompt goes through f16 chunks on an idle server and through exact windows
when another slot decodes, and a prefix-reuse rewind rebuilds the ring with exact windows over an f16 store. So
the temperature-0 output of a long prompt can depend on server load. The "Match Strata" contract permits it;
`QX_DS_PREFILL_EXACT=1` removes it. Presupposition row 13 says so now.

## 2026-11-11: Prompt chunks: f16 attention, filled f16 GEMM launches, the full stack on the kept rows only (mixed chunks), Engram rows off the critical path — set D 25.447 -> 19.770 (prefill256k 4.7 -> 2.2); three-slot 256k server, 13,283-token prompt 26.8 s -> 13.0 s (495 -> 1,024 tok/s); promotion run `perf/results/2026-11-11/setd-promo8-032500` (adjudication below)

Trees: 09e123f (attention, GEMM planes, Engram thread, keep split) and a41128e (mixed chunks, the breaker's
fixes); binaries chunk92 = a41128e clean (qx-ds-check 514c699145bd, quixiinfer 124920a690f2).
Hypothesis (from the nsys table of the promoted tree, `~/qx-chains/nsys2`, 16k prompt, 37.2 s of kernel time):
`qx_gemm16` 8.25 s, of which 4.6 s were 25,600 launches of 16 blocks (the eight output-projection groups, one
launch each: a block's time is its K loop, 44 us per 1,024 of K, however few blocks run) and 1.7 s the 8- and
20-block launches of `g_x`; the exact attention kernels 9.4 s; the Engram row reads 0.4-1.0 s per 4,096-row chunk
with the card idle; and the final chunk ran all 40 layers on every row where only the last `prompt_keep` (2,796)
need them.

What changed (prompt chunks only; decode, windows and `QX_DS_PREFILL_EXACT=1` chunks keep the exact kernels):
1. `qx_gemm16` takes planes (groups x K slices): the eight `wo_a` groups in one launch, products of few output rows
   K-sliced to about 320 blocks, the slices' partials added in order by `qx_gemm16_sum` (the same bits every run;
   a row's bits do not depend on the slab or chunk it is in). 8.25 s -> 1.6 s. `QX_DS_GEMM16_BLOCKS=0` never slices.
2. `qx_ds_attn16`: the chunk's attention on f16 tensor cores, 16 heads x 16 keys per step with a running softmax
   (m = sink, l = 1 at the start). 9.4 s -> 1.6 s. `QX_DS_ATTN16=0` keeps the exact kernels.
3. Mixed chunks (`forward_chunk_keep`): the rows before the kept ones stop at the last KV source inside the same
   chunk, so the layers above run on the kept rows only and the lower layers stream their experts once. A prompt
   longer than a chunk runs KV-only chunks up to the kept rows. `QX_DS_CHUNK_MIXED=0`: a KV-only chunk and a chunk
   of the kept rows (the plan of 09e123f). The server and `qx-ds-check` plan the same way; `qx-ds-check` adds its
   tail to the keep (the tail's first rows are compared bit for bit).
4. A chunk's Engram rows on a thread (direct page reads, four rings), each engram layer (1 and 14) waiting for
   its rows. What I tried first and measured: six buffered rings, twelve threads of positioned reads, direct
   reads in line: the same 0.4-1.0 s each, so the reads were not limited by how they were issued; they now
   overlap the card's work. `QX_DS_ENGRAM_THREADS=0` gathers in line.
5. Harness: prefill256k's value is the mean over its last 8,192 rows (`QX_DS_LAST=8192`): the last 8,192 rows
   were one full-stack chunk until today, so "the last 32" was that chunk's per-row time; with the full stack on
   the kept rows only the per-row time differs inside the chunk. Like for like with the baseline's 4.7: the
   baseline's own log reads `chunk of 8192 rows at 253952: 38425 ms (4.69 ms per row)`, one uniform full-stack
   chunk, so its mean over the last 8,192 rows is the 4.69 its "last 32" printed. (I also ran the old binaries,
   chunk81, under today's harness: they predate `QX_DS_LAST` and ignore it, printing `last 32: 4.7` with the chunk
   at 38,430 ms; so that run shows the chunk time again, not the new harness value. The adjudicator's point.)
   The whole case's mean, independent of the span: 59.0 s -> 32.4 s for the 16,384 rows, 3.6 -> 2.0 ms per token.
   `perf/perf.md`, the scoreboard's set D bullet and `goal-prompt.md` say "the last 8,192 rows" now; the row in
   `results.jsonl` still carries the old field name `ms_per_token_last32`.

Measurements (single runs unless said; `~/qx-chains/log.out` tags FILL2, BG, AT, V8, BRK, BRK2, SRV, V9):

|---|--:|--:|--:|
| 16k prompt, steady 4,096-row KV-only chunk | 6.2 s | 3.3 s | 2.9 s (chunk90/91: 09e123f plus the then-uncommitted mixed chunks) |
| three-slot 256k server, 13,283-token prompt (`req:` prompt seconds) | 26.84 s | 16.04, 16.06 s | 12.97 s |
| the same, tok/s | 495 | 828 | 1,024 |
| server, 3,293-token prompt | | split plan 8.32 s (chunk91 with `QX_DS_CHUNK_MIXED=0`) | 5.94 s (no skip: 6.94 s, chunk91) |
| prefill256k (ms per token over the last 8,192 rows) | 4.7 | 2.5 | 2.2 |
| the last 8,192 rows' wall time at 256k | 38.4 s | 20.1 s | 18.4 s (promotion run; 18.7 s in the run with a 64-row tail) |
| the KV-only chunk before them (8,192 rows at 245,760) | 20.6 s | 14.9 s | 14.1 s (promotion run; 14.8 s with the tail) |
Of prefill256k's 4.7 -> 2.2, most is the full stack running on 2,796 rows instead of 8,192 (the breaker's point);
the kernels' own share shows in the KV-only chunk, which does the same work in both trees: 20.6 -> 14.1 s (-32%).
The kernel table after (09e123f, `~/qx-chains/nsys3`): `qx_moe_wait_word` 25.7 s (the expert kernels waiting for
their staged records, over eight streams), experts 16 s, attention 1.83 s (`qx_ds_attn16` 1.59 s plus the exact
kernels of the dry passes), `qx_gemm16` 1.58 s, the Engram
projection (`qx_mgemv_t8z`, exact, 25,600 x 6,144) 1.3 s. Per layer of a steady chunk (125 ms): slab kernels 42
ms, experts 46-60 ms, copies busy 60-80 ms (3.3 GB): the experts' streaming and arithmetic are what is left.
A full-stack pass costs about 6 s however few its rows (2,774 rows 6.8 s, 5,544 rows 6.85 s): 40 layers x ~300
experts x 9.5 MB to stream. That floor is the next target.

The breaker (fresh context, read-only review of 09e123f, then its tests run by me):
- Found by its test B: a chunk whose first slab is narrower than a later one ran the wider slabs' dry passes on
  the first slab's token arrays, so they wrote ring and store cells of stale positions: 0/64 rows on the 5-row
  case (chunk90). Introduced by the mixed chunk (before it a chunk's first slab was its widest). Fixed: the
  arrays cover the widest slab; 64/64 (chunk91).
- The Engram thread inherited the engine thread's one-CPU mask: the server waited 373-480 ms at engram layer 1.
  Fixed (every CPU; nothing in it spins): 60-215 ms in the V9 server.
- A failed Engram read would have left the engine waiting forever: the wait now asserts the thread is alive.
- Its metric objection is answered in point 5 above; its cost objection (the split streamed the lower layers'
  experts twice: NVMe records +29% on the 13.3k prompt, prompts of 3-6k tokens slower than no skip) is what the
  mixed chunk removes (3,293 tokens: 5.94 s against 6.94 s without the skip).
- Checked by it and found sound: the keep derivation (from existing dumps: exactness starts 19 x 127 rows after
  the boundary; 2,796 leaves 255 spare), the info ring's stale words (every `qx_gemm16` call passes [7]),
  `blas_map`'s lifetime, the scratch sizes (partials 16.8 MB against a worst need of 12.6), the direct reads (rows
  never straddle a page), the attention kernel's layouts, masking, ring wrap and slot indexing.

Costs and what is not covered:
- Accuracy against the promoted tree, at the 256k configuration, the same 64 rows against the same exact dump:
  KL mean 0.0048 -> 0.0085, max 0.044 -> 0.115, top-1 64/64 -> 63/64 (the attention is f16 now too). Inside the
  bound, and about twice the distance from the exact path that the promoted tree had.
- With two long prompts at once both wait: chunks run only while no slot decodes, so slot 1's prompt went on in
  windows once slot 0 decoded. 10,031 tokens on slot 1: 54.8 s on the f16 path (chunk91), 64.9 s (chunk91) and
  81 s (chunk92) with the exact knob (different schedules); slot 0's 13,283 tokens took 21.8 s beside it on the
  f16 path against 12.97 s alone. That policy is older than this work; it is now the largest prompt-side cost
  under load.
- The prefill256k log's driver counters changed (`serve 40 calls`, `spec 39/40` -> `serve 200 calls`, `spec
  199/200`): not looked into.
- f16 `QX_DS_GEMM16=cublas` was not re-run on these trees (the code path compiles; the knob stays opt-in).
- `qx-ds-multi` all rows: not run (no window, scheduler or job-path change).
- top-1 on the 15,153-token case is 95.3% over 64 rows, at the bound; the larger samples read 97.7-98.4%.
- The server's long-prompt numbers are single sessions (two for 09e123f: 16.04 and 16.06 s).

Against Strata, same 16,384 ids, same box state, alternating runs (2026-11-11 03:43-03:48, tag H2H; Strata's
own prompt timer, a fresh process each run): Strata 841 and 880 tok/s (19.5 and 18.6 s). Ours as a fresh process

**ahead of those Strata runs with our server warm** (different text, and Strata's process was fresh each time: a

tok/s on 2026-11-10; the 256k server decodes at 27.05 tok/s at concurrency 1 and 10.99 per request at

## 2026-11-11 (morning): The tokens right after a long prompt: evictions at a chunk's tick capped — LANDED candidate (64 tokens after a 13k prompt 3.76 -> 3.55 s, after a second one 3.33 -> 2.72 s; scored cases unchanged by construction); four tier ideas rejected; Strata like for like

Trees: 1ac3e01 (the cap on every tick: rejected by its own promotion-style run), ec8cb28 (the cap on chunk ticks
only; binaries chunk104: qx-ds-check 6004c15416fb, quixiinfer 8f509760f785).
The question: Strata's first 8 tokens after a 16k prompt run at 14.2-14.4 tok/s; ours in the three-slot 256k
server at 8-11, and the 64 tokens after a 13.3k prompt take 3.70 s (57.8 ms each against ~37 steady).
What the server's first steps after the prompt look like (new verbose lines `post-prompt step N`, chunk97): 188,
164, 158, 135, 90, 101, 65, 56 ms; of a step's 240 selections the first step finds 27 in VRAM (11%), 57 staged,
94 in RAM and **62 on NVMe only**; by step 7: 99 / 50 / 82 / 9. The whole excess is the tiers.

Tried, measured, rejected (each a same-binary A/B in the three-slot 256k server unless said; patches kept in
`~/qx-chains/`):
1. Filling the tail's empty slots at the end of the prompt with the hottest RAM records (`flex-fill.patch`, tag
   FILLV): empty slots 102 -> 0, the 64 tokens 3.70 s both ways.
2. The chunk's selection counts as a per-token rate in the scores, plus 1 (`chunk-scores-fill.patch`, tags RAMP2,
   POST; today a chunk's counts are decayed by its rows at its own tick, 0.5^32 for 8,192): `qx-ds-check`, 16k
   prompt, steps 2-9 after it 50.4 -> 47.2 ms; server 3.70 -> 3.81 s, loadgen slightly worse. No.
3. The layer's hottest NVMe-only records of the prompt promoted to RAM during the chunk (12 or 32 per layer; tag
   RPR): RAM promotions 997 -> 1,614 -> 2,196 by the end of the prompt, NVMe selections on the first step 36 -> 38
   -> 37. The decode's misses are not on the records the prompt used most: the reply (a chat turn about the
   text) routes differently from the text.
4. Saving the tail's residents that have no RAM copy into RAM when the tail is taken (`evict-cap+flex-save.patch`,
   tag SAVE): 55 records saved, empty slots after the prompt 77 -> 22, the first steps and the 64 tokens unchanged
   (3.45 / 3.47 s).
5. The eviction cap on every tick (1ac3e01; promotion-style run `setd-promo9-052143`): prefill128 10.5 -> 15.8,
   decode256k 38.6 -> 39.4, serve256k1 36.97 -> 39.22, serve256k3 90.99 -> 89.13. Window and decode ticks need
   their bursts (the next ticks' budgets refill them). The breaker had predicted both: evictions were never
   bounded by the fill budget (a victim with no free slot never entered `plan`), so the cap bound on single steps
   too, and a 128-token window got 32 evictions.

What landed (ec8cb28): at a prompt chunk's tick (`dt` over a window's width) evictions are capped at the per-token
budget (`QX_DS_EVICT_CAP`, 0 = none). A victim's slot is quarantined two ticks, so its candidate enters VRAM only
if another slot is free, and during a prompt the flex tail holds the free slots: a chunk's tick evicted hundreds
of residents and promoted none, and the decode ticks after it refill at the per-token budget.
| three-slot 256k server, same binary (chunk103 = the code of ec8cb28), A B B A | no cap | cap |
|---|--:|--:|
| 64 tokens after the first 13,284-token prompt | 3.76, 3.76 s | 3.57, 3.54 s (-5.6%) |
| 64 tokens after a second, different 13,242-token prompt | 3.33, 3.33 s | 2.75, 2.70 s (-18%) |
| its first four steps | 187, 167, 137, 84 ms | 122, 70, 44, 31 ms |
| loadgen after them, concurrency 1: per-request tok/s; TTFT median | 24.46, 24.45; 1.54 s | 24.21, 24.60; 1.64, 1.65 s |
| loadgen, concurrency 3: per-request tok/s | 11.27, 11.28 | 11.25, 11.28 |
| the two generated messages (64 tokens each) | identical in all four sessions | |
With the server's default budget (`QX_DS_PROMOS` unset: 4 a token; chunk104, one session each): 4.65 -> 3.96 s and
3.71 -> 3.05 s; loadgen at 1: 22.04 -> 21.62 tok/s per request, TTFT 1.59 -> 1.69 s.
Cost: the short requests that follow a long prompt start about 0.1 s later (TTFT 1.54 -> 1.64 s; fewer empty
slots for the next text's candidates), and at the default budget their decode reads 2% slower in the one session.
Scored cases, unchanged by construction (no tick of a scored decode or window has `dt` over 128; prefill256k's
chunk ticks are capped but nothing after them is timed): promotion-style run `setd-promo10-055704` on chunk104:

## 2026-11-12: Kernel modules loaded once per source with their PTX on disk; a chunk job's NVMe reads beside its RAM copies — set D 19.770 -> 19.166 (prefill256k 2.241 -> 2.003 unrounded, about three quarters of it compiles that sat inside the measured chunk); a fresh process's 16k prompt 710-739 -> 1,132-1,158 tok/s; warmed 2,048-token prompt 4.81 -> 3.83 s; promotion run `perf/results/2026-11-12/setd-promo13-161136` (adjudication below)

Tree: the promotion run was made on 967476e plus a dirty working tree (diff_sha 955828554b0f), committed afterwards
as 24090ba with an identical diff; binaries chunk110 (qx-ds-check a78bd7bb6b73,
quixiinfer 8c2ca42d5df6). Earlier builds of the same work: chunk105 (module sharing only), chunk107 (+ overlap),
chunk109 (+ the first breaker's fixes), chunk110 (+ the second breaker's two hardening notes).

### 1. Cold start (backlog item 5)
Measured first (chunk104, a fresh `qx-ds-check`, 16,384 tokens, verbose with wall-clock stamps, `cold-a.log`): 44
`nvrtc compile` lines, 41 of them ahead of the first chunk's line (inside its timed span, 448.365 to 453.5 s, with
six raw-launcher compiles after them: 6.4 s), two inside the second chunk and one in the tail. CubeCL compiles and loads a "precompiled" source once per kernel id (entry point x block size); our sources
hold many entry points, so `mgemv.cu` (97 MB of PTX) was compiled and loaded eight times at 0.75-1.3 s each, and
the raw launcher compiled its own copy of every module on top.
Change (vendored `third_party/cubecl-cuda`, `qx-gpu`):
- one loaded module per source text (`QX_MODULES`, keyed by the FNV-1a of the source); every kernel id takes its
  function from it; `qx_gpu::raw` reads and fills the same table (`QX_MODULE_SHARE=0`: the old form);
- the PTX on disk in `~/.cache/quixiinfer/ptx/<fnv of source>-<hash of the options and NVRTC's version>.ptx`
  (`QX_PTX_CACHE=0` disables), stored only after the driver loaded it, dropped and recompiled when the driver
  refuses it (not on out-of-memory);
- the NVRTC program is destroyed after its PTX is taken (upstream leaked it: ~63 MiB per compile, 2026-11-06).

| fresh process, 16k prompt (chunk 1 / chunk 2 ms; tool timer) | runs |
|---|---|
| chunk104 | 11,683 / 9,667 (710 tok/s); 10,715 / 9,731 (733); 10,801 / 9,684 (739) |
| new, `QX_MODULE_SHARE=0 QX_PTX_CACHE=0` (chunk105) | 11,032 / 9,721 (728) |
| chunk105, cache filled | 5,521 / 8,170 (1,117); 5,543 / 8,169 (1,117); 5,832 / 8,174 (1,093); one outlier 9,405 / 8,179 (881) |
| chunk105, cache empty (first run after a kernel edit) | 7,491 / 8,171 (978) |
| chunk109 (with the overlap of part 2), six in a row | 5,531-5,827 / 7,634-7,664 (1,132-1,158) |

Strata on the same ids: 841-880 tok/s (measured 2026-11-11, not rerun today). The outlier (run b2) spent 3.7 s between the start of the chunk and the
first kernel line, before any code this change touches; the breaker found the same gap at 0.6 s in an old run;
none in the six repeats on chunk109. Not explained.
Side effects: the server's warm-up 24.5-25.0 -> 12.0-12.1 s; device memory free after the warm-up of a three-slot
256k server 0.45 -> 1.21 GiB (the VRAM tier is cut before any kernel loads and is unchanged: 12.3 GiB); a warmed
process's first prompt chunk also gains (5,993 -> 4,784 ms: the six raw compiles were paid at the first prompt).
A first run after a kernel source edit pays the compile and a 113 MB write: prime the cache (run once) before
timing a binary with edited kernels; `module <hash>: PTX from the cache | compiled` and `raw: ... PTX from the
cache | compiled` under `QX_DS_VERBOSE` say which happened. The cache directory is benchmark state from now on
(every module of the promotion run loaded from it); nothing prunes it (218 MB for two generations of sources).

### 2. A chunk job's NVMe reads beside its RAM copies (backlog item 3)
Measured first (`QX_DS_CHUNK_TIMING=1`, a sync after each layer's experts; chunk106): a warmed 2,040-row
full-stack chunk spends 24 ms per layer in the slab passes and 90-160 ms in the experts, rising with the layer;
16k: layers 20-39 on 2,796 rows, slabs 35 ms (80 on every fourth layer, 126 on layer 20), experts 46-115 ms. Per
layer about 96 of the ~335 staged copies (prefetched picks included, used or not; 306 distinct experts are used)
come from NVMe (lane counters of a later instrumented build, exp1:
9,573 staged from RAM and 3,839 from NVMe in the 2,040-row chunk; 3,467 records are NVMe-only), and
`serve_chunk` read them first, in blocking batches of 48, each
followed by a wait for its copies, and only then copied the RAM records: disk and link took turns.
Change (`driver.rs`, `QX_DS_CHUNK_OVERLAP=0` restores the old form): the next run of NVMe records (at most half
the class's 64 landing places) is submitted before the records ahead of it are staged and collected when its turn
comes; the prefetch pick puts a share of the RAM records ahead of each NVMe batch; a batch's copies are not waited
for (two event sets, used in turn: before a batch's reads are submitted the set recorded two batches ago is waited
for); `serve()` waits for every chunk staging copy before it touches the landing places or the staging area.

| warmed `qx-ds-check`, on and off on the same binary, alternating (chunk107; the third 2k pair chunk110) | overlap on | off |
|---|--:|--:|
| 2,048 tokens (one full-stack chunk), ms | 3,809; 3,822; 3,838 | 4,805; 4,806; 4,809 |
| 16,384 tokens (KV-only chunk + mixed chunk), ms | 4,745 + 7,277 | 4,840 + 7,799 |
| the breaker's runs (chunk109): 2,048 / 264 / 40 tokens | 3,833 / 3,635 / 4,273 | 4,802 / 4,406 / 4,979 |

4,795 + 7,302; NVMe wait unchanged (1,480 against 1,478 ms). Flat, not landed. The staging lane (one thread, the

A serve256k session runs no chunk job (0 `chunk of` lines in all of today's logs), so the overlap code is not
executed in it. The two level-1 requests are the same two prompts in every session, and each lands in one of two
modes: 9.0-9.7 s or 10.2-10.9 s for 256 tokens. The counters of the request tell the modes apart: slow =
31,600-35,600 VRAM hits, ~1,150-1,480 NVMe reads, ~1,500 RAM promotions; fast = 36,400-39,900 VRAM hits, 685-955
NVMe reads. The tier the six warm-up requests leave (three at a time, before level 1) decides it. Counts of slow
requests (10.0 s or more), recomputed by the adjudicator from the `req:` lines:

| chunk105 | 1 | 1 of 2 | 0 |
| chunk109 | 6 | 8 of 12 (both slow in the two 41.3 sessions) | 0 |
| chunk110 | 4 | 4 of 8 | 0 |

## 2026-11-12 (evening): A prefetch job skips the picks the routing leaves without rows; NVMe batches of 8 over all the landing places — LANDED (warmed prompt chunk: 2,048 tokens 3.81-3.84 -> 3.22-3.26 s, 264 tokens 3.63 -> 1.84 s (one pair); a forced 32-row chunk in `qx-ds-check` 4.50 -> 1.88 s (one pair, not a server path); set D within noise, 19.213 against 19.166); run `perf/results/2026-11-12/setd-promo15-215438` (adjudication below)

Tree: 30100a2 plus a dirty working tree (diff_sha 2f82331319e9), committed with this entry; binaries
`~/qx-bins/chunk113` (qx-ds-check d3d90a47d3c3, quixiinfer a6d44b86d70f, qx-ds-multi 13b74d8190b0, qx-ds-replay
e15babcd04d9). Earlier builds of the same work: exp3-exp10 (worktree `~/qx-wt/exp`), chunk111 (the knob was named
`QX_DS_CHUNK_BATCH`, which the engine already reads for the batched grouped kernels), chunk112 (renamed
`QX_DS_CHUNK_READS`), chunk113 (chunk112 with the doc comment's numbers corrected: no code difference).

### Correction to the entry before this one
"~2,060 ms inside the copy calls ... the link's speed" was an attribution by subtraction and is wrong. Counters
around each call (exp3, the 2,040-row chunk of a warmed 2,048-token prompt, 3,926 ms, driver busy 3,641): the copy
calls take 56 ms; `fetch_submit` (the io_uring submit) blocks 1,988 ms; `fetch_wait` 1,488 ms; landing places
56 ms; a ring slot 50 ms. The drive's largest request is 128 KiB (`max_hw_sectors_kb`), so a 9.5 MB record is 74
requests and a batch of 32 is ~2,400 against 1,023 tags: the submit blocks until the queue drains. The disk is
busy ~3.5 of the chunk's 3.9 s at ~10.4 GB/s; `fio` on the pack alone (O_DIRECT, 9,728 KiB blocks, random) reads
11.1 GB/s at depth 8 and 11.8 at 32. A short prompt is bounded by the disk, not the link (presupposition 42 revised).

### What the disk was reading
The prefetch job picks up to 256 non-resident experts of a layer before the routing is known, NVMe-tier first.
Of the 2,040-row chunk's 3,839 NVMe reads, 2,356 were of experts the chunk routes to (`QX_DS_CHUNK_PREFETCH=0`
reads exactly those and takes 4,371 ms: no head start); of 10,240 picks, 12,259 - 4,313 = ~7,950 were used.

### Change (`driver.rs`, `mod.rs`)
- The engine publishes a layer's routing (which experts have rows) as soon as it has the lists
  (`Shared::chunk_used`, tagged with the job's generation); the prefetch job, still staging its pick, skips every
  pick without rows from then on: no read, no copy, no ring slot, no word (the engine only marks an unused
  pick's ordinal, as before). `QX_DS_CHUNK_SKIP=0`: stage them all.
- NVMe batches of 8 records instead of 32 (`QX_DS_CHUNK_READS`): a batch is read blind once submitted, and 8
  records are ~600 requests in flight, the disk's full rate. A batch passes over skipped picks.
- Up to eight event sets instead of two (`QX_DS_CHUNK_SETS`): batch j waits for the copies of batch j - nsets
  before its reads go out (nsets x batch <= the class's 64 landing places, handed out in rotation), so the disk
  no longer waits for the copies of the batch just before (410-420 ms of the chunk with two sets and batches of 8).
- Verbose: a `chunk lane:` line per chunk (driver busy, ring-slot wait, NVMe wait and submit, landing places,
  copy calls, records staged per tier, picks skipped).

| warmed `qx-ds-check`, chunk ms (alternated; chunk113 unless noted) | new | chunk110 | new with `READS=32 SETS=2 SKIP=0` |
|---|--:|--:|--:|
| 2,048 tokens | 3,239; 3,216 (chunk112 3,262; chunk111 3,259, 3,238) | 3,813 (3,842) | 3,847 (3,863) |
| 16,384 tokens: KV-only chunk + mixed chunk | 4,818 + 6,964 | 4,758 + 7,277 | |
| 264 tokens (chunk112; one pair, old binary first) | 1,840 | 3,634 | |
| 40 tokens, `QX_DS_WARM=0`: not warmed, a forced 32-row chunk (chunk112; one pair, old binary first) | 1,882 | 4,498 | |

### Adjudication (fresh context): LANDED
Gate recomputed (19.166 -> 19.213, every scored case within noise or under the bar; serve256k1's -5.0% is the
two-mode row, not a win); the landing bar (8%) is cleared on the targeted case (2k chunk -15%, ranges disjoint);
ds-checks and every dump re-compared; chunk112 and chunk113 are byte-identical in `.text`, `.rodata`, `.data`
(27 panic line numbers differ), so chunk112's evidence stands for chunk113. No promotion, no scoreboard row. Its
twelve text corrections are applied above (the 40-token row is not a server path; preflight; what the exact-knob
checks do not cover).

### Breaker (fresh context, on chunk111's diff; code reading, no runs)
No defect in the diff: landing-place reuse holds for every `READS` 1-32 and `SETS` 2-8 (all classes have 64
places; only batches j - nsets + 1 .. j - 1 can have copies not waited for); a skipped pick has an empty list by
the same `lists` the launches use, so it is in neither `rest` nor `extra_scores`; the generation is re-checked
under the lock; ring ordinals stay monotone; no path to the panic, to double staging or to an unstaged record.
Its notes, handled: batch 8 without skips on long chunks (measured above: no cost at 256k); the doc comment's
numbers came from three binaries (re-measured on chunk112); short prompts untested (264 and 40 tokens: identical,
and the largest gains); `QX_DS_CHUNK_BATCH` read twice (renamed). Pre-existing and open: `promote_one` on the
first idle iteration after a chunk job may evict a RAM record whose staging copy is still queued (the victim
filter excludes only `inflight` / `pre_inflight`); narrow (the class's coldest RAM record with a copy queued);
needs a counter on a replayed three-slot schedule.

### Tried and not landed
- A cap on the RAM records of the prefetch (`QX_DS_CHUNK_PRE_RAM`, exp4): 0: 3,997 ms; 64: 3,872; all: 3,891-3,926.
- The skip with batches of 32 and one batch of read-ahead (exp5): 3,740-3,751 against 3,877-3,911 off; only 178
  NVMe reads cancelled (all of a layer's batches are submitted before its routing arrives).
- Read-ahead by one batch with batches of 8 (exp8/exp9): 3,285-3,325 against 3,237-3,261 without; removed.
- Registered io_uring buffers for the landing places (exp7): registration fails with ENOMEM under the 8 MiB
  memlock limit (hard limit: raising it needs root), and with batches of 8 the submits take 0.43 s for 2,965
  records, so the per-read page pinning is not what blocked.
- Predicting a layer's NVMe-tier use from the previous chunk of the same prompt (exp9, 16k in 2,048-row chunks,
  `QX_DS_SKIP=0`): of the NVMe-tier experts used before, 68% are used now against a base rate of 50% (1,325 of
  1,939; later chunks 47-69%). Too weak to order the picks by. The same run: the NVMe-tier experts a chunk uses
  fall 2,355 -> 924 over the eight chunks (the tiers adapt to the prompt), chunks 3.0-3.3 s each.
- One run of the session (`sets1-b16-noahead`) died at start with CUDA out of memory: 0.46 GiB free after load
  against 2.14-2.15 in the others (one read 2.97); `time1.sh`'s gate can pass while the previous context is still tearing down
  (the later chains wait 3 s more; all nine runs of chain BRK1 read 2.14).

What is left of a 2k chunk: ~700 NVMe reads of experts not used (the blind part of each layer's prefetch) and
2,356 that are (22 GB: 2.0 s at the disk's rate, against a chunk of 3.2 s).

## 2026-11-13: Long prompt stretches run in chunks beside decoders too — LANDED (a 10,031-token prompt arriving beside a reply 196.6 -> 9.0 s and that reply over 197 s (cut at 93 of 176 tokens by the client's 200 s limit) -> 18.7 s; two long prompts at once: the second 72.0 -> 15.4 s, the first's 64 reply tokens 64.9 -> 9.1 s; set D within noise, 19.353 and 19.224 against 19.166); runs `perf/results/2026-11-13/setd-promo17-032618`, `setd-promo18-034659` (adjudication below)

Tree: 9725a58 plus `crates/qx-serve/src/worker_ds.rs` (diff_sha 9373cccaff3b), committed with this entry;
binaries `~/qx-bins/chunk115`: quixiinfer 08abaf1aada9; qx-ds-check d3d90a47d3c3, qx-ds-multi 13b74d8190b0 and
qx-ds-replay e15babcd04d9 are chunk113's files (no engine source changed). Earlier builds of the same work:
`besd1` (worktree, the first form), chunk114 (the same code as chunk115 before the comment edits and before the
flex-tail release at a window's end, below).

### The question
The scheduler sent a prompt through the chunk path only while no request decoded ("decoders would wait seconds
behind a chunk"). A long prompt arriving beside a decoder went through 128-wide full-stack windows shared with
the decoders, with the flex tail held for the earlier chunks: 2.5 s per window in the three-slot 256k server.
Backlog item 1 since 2026-11-11 (slot 1's 10,031-token prompt 54.8-83 s beside a 13k prompt).

### Change (`worker_ds.rs` only)
- A prompt stretch of at least max(window, 256) tokens always runs in chunks. While a request decodes, the
  decoders get `QX_DS_CHUNK_GAP` window steps of their own between two chunks (default 4; 0 measured the same
  on besd1: second prompt 14.9 against 15.4 s), and a stretch waiting for its chunk stays out of those windows.
  `QX_DS_CHUNK_BESIDE=0`: the old policy.
- A prompt that ends in a window gives the flex tail back once no stretch waits for a chunk (a no-op unless the
  tail is held: with `QX_DS_SKIP=0` or a `QX_DS_CHUNK` under the kept rows a remainder under 256 tokens finished
  in windows and the tail stayed out until a later prompt; breaker's note, pre-existing, non-default).

Three-slot server, `--ctx 262144 --window 128`, `QX_DS_VRAM=20 QX_DS_RAM=95`, 64-token replies unless noted,
one run each (chunk115 unless noted; old = chunk113):

| case | old | new |
|---|--:|--:|
| two prompts 0.3 s apart (13,283 and 10,031 tokens): second prompt | 72.0 s (`BESIDE=0` on besd1: 72.1) | 15.4 s (besd1: 15.4; besd1 at gap 0: 14.9) |
| the first request's 64 reply tokens | 64.9 s (65.1) | 9.1 s |
| the same, `QX_DS_PREFILL_EXACT=1`: second prompt / first's reply | 83.2 / 65.8 s | 30.1 / 13.5 s (chunk114) |
| a 10,031-token prompt arriving 5 s after a 1,638-token request (mid-reply): the prompt | 196.6 s | 9.0 s (alone 8.8) |
| that request's reply (176 tokens; alone 8.0 s) | 197 s for 93 tokens, then the client's 200 s limit | 18.7 s |
| a 413-token prompt arriving mid-reply: the prompt | 23.9 s (`BESIDE=0`) | 2.4 s |
| that request's 176-token reply | 33.6 s (`BESIDE=0`) | 12.4 s |

What a decoder pays: it waits one chunk at a time (4.3-4.5 s for the chunks a decoder waited behind, 2,796 and
7,235 rows; the 5,091-row full-stack chunk is 5.9 s; 8.6-9.3 s with the exact knob; a chunk at 250k positions
is 9.3 s KV-only and 16.1 s full stack in prefill256k's `qx-ds-check` run, not measured beside a decoder), with
4 tokens between two chunks. The reply beside the 10k prompt takes 18.7 s against 8.0 alone: two chunks (4.5 + 4.3 s) and slower
steps while the tail is out. The longest gap between two stream deltas was not timed; it is one chunk.
In default mode a prompt's reply no longer depends on whether a decoder was running when it arrived (it did:
exact windows beside a decoder, f16 chunks alone); the second prompt's reply in the two-prompt case therefore
differs from the old binary's (expected, inside the contract: prompt chunks KL-bounded, decode exact).

### Exactness (chunk115 unless noted; `QX_DS_SCHED_TRACE` row hashes compared with `trace_slot.py` / `trace_cmp.py`)
- Exact knob, two prompts at once against the old binary's trace (`t0`), on besd1 and on chunk114 (not rerun on
  chunk115): 60/60 of the last rows of both slots (64/64, breaker).
- Exact knob, request A alone against A beside B: A's chunk row and 97 decode rows 98/98 at gap 4 (chunk114;
  recomputed with `trace_slot.py`; the log line reads 190/190, `trace_cmp.py` with n=190), gap 1 and gap 3; B's 64 rows equal across the three gaps; A's text identical.
- Exact knob, three requests (A, B after 5 s, a 79-token C one second later, which runs in windows while B's
  stretch is held and the tail is out): A 98/98 against alone, B 64/64, C's last 60 rows equal to C alone, text
  identical.
- Default mode: A alone against beside B 170/170 rows, text identical; B alone against B beside A 60/60, text
  identical.

- The comment in `worker_ds.rs` quotes chunk114's figures (9.1 / 18.9 s); chunk115 reads 9.03 / 18.66. The

binaries d3d90a47d3c3 / 08abaf1aada9): decode256k 38.8, 39.0 (pool 1,437 and 1,446 ms: the baseline's mode),

### Breaker (fresh context, on chunk114's diff; code reading, no runs)
No defect: `forward_chunk` saves and restores what a chunk shares with the windows, stores and rings are per
slot; grammars are touched only where a request samples; a request cannot leave `running` with a stretch
pending; with defaults a non-final chunk leaves at least the kept rows (2,796), so no remainder under 256;
`chunk_rows == 0`, one slot and `BESIDE=0` reduce to the old schedule. Its notes, handled: other interleaves
(gap 1, gap 3, a third short prompt: above); default-mode row hashes (above); the two-prompt figures came from
besd1 (re-measured on chunk115: the same); a ~300-token stretch beside a decoder unmeasured (the 413-token rows);
the tail leak (released at a window's end now); the `old1` / `old2` guard directories record the dirty tree but
ran chunk113 (NOTE.txt in each).

### Not from this change, open
- One server start of chunk114 (`v16-db`, of ~30 starts of the new binaries) panicked in the warm-up's graph
  captures before any request: "No room for 56 bytes in the memory held, and no page may be added (a graph
  capture is recording)" at the 48-token width (cubecl-runtime `PageUpdateForbidden`; message read from the
  log before the rerun overwrote it: in no kept file). Start-up code is
  unchanged; six start-only launches of chunk115 were clean; the log was overwritten by the rerun. The process
  stayed up with a dead engine thread (the HTTP side answers nothing): it should exit.
- Windows beside a decoder cost 2.5 s for 127 prompt tokens (and ~6 s each in the 413-token `BESIDE=0` run: 23.9 s for four windows):
  stretches under 256 tokens still take them (a 79-token prompt beside a reply and a held stretch: 3.1 s with
  the exact knob; alone as the first request of a fresh server 3.6 s: no cost of being beside shown).

### Adjudication (fresh context)
HOLD, then LANDED after `setd-promo18-034659` (not a promotion: no scoreboard row, the baseline stays 19.166).
The hold was procedural: chains V16-V18 wrote no preflight line, promo17's decode256k read in two modes (41.1 /
38.7) and only one identity session existed on chunk115. Verified: the gate arithmetic of both runs; the three
ds-checks binaries byte-identical to chunk113's and no engine source changed; every table figure and every hash
and text comparison recomputed from the traces and logs (A 98/98 and 544/544 over all rows at gap 4, C 142/142,
default A 177/177, B 64/64); `worker_ds.rs` equal to the run directories' diff; old = chunk113 with the same
prompts, offsets and settings. The landing bar (8%) is cleared on the targeted cases by factors of 4.7 to 22.
Its corrections are applied above (the old reply never finished: cut at 93 of 176 tokens; the two gate "wins"
are two-mode rows; which figures are besd1's or chunk114's).

## 2026-11-13 (morning): Prompt stretches of 64 tokens or more run in chunks (exact under 256 rows, no blind prefetch there); a RAM place staged by a chunk is settled before it changes hands; a dead engine thread ends the server — LANDED (fresh three-slot 256k server, prompt time: 79 tokens 3.58 -> 1.54 s, 128: 7.02 -> 1.78, 186: 8.52 -> 2.22, 232: 9.35 -> 2.52, replies bit-identical; set D within noise, 19.164 against 19.166); run `perf/results/2026-11-13/setd-promo25-074000` (adjudication below)

Tree: 6c50cde plus `driver.rs`, `mod.rs`, `worker_ds.rs` (`git diff -- crates` sha 8d0fb658ea88), committed with
this entry; binaries `~/qx-bins/chunk120` (qx-ds-check 3ad605200855, quixiinfer 8d6080d8d5c0, qx-ds-multi
66e901c7895b, qx-ds-replay 7fb63b6c6260; `build-chunk120.log`). Earlier builds of the same work, each superseded
after a breaker or adjudicator note: exp11-exp13 (worktree), chunk116 (guard, abort on panic, `CHUNK_MIN` 64),
chunk117 (capture lock in `chunk_settle`, `chunk_tick`, `_exit`), chunk118 (`CHUNK_PREFETCH_MIN`), chunk119 (panic
hook, `chunk_used_gen` zeroed, the test knob; validated by chain V23, held by the adjudicator), chunk120
(`CHUNK_F16_MIN`). Chains V20-V22 were stopped (screening); V25 is the validation of chunk120.

### What was measured first
- **Windows are the slow path for a short prompt.** Fresh three-slot 256k server, distinct prompts in sequence:
  79 tokens 3.6 s, 128: 6.8-7.3 s, 186: 8.5-9.6 s, 232: 9.2-9.6 s in windows (the first request reads 5,951 NVMe
  records in windows against 2,823 in a chunk on chunk120: a full-stack window fetches its misses per window). The same
  prompts through the chunk path (`QX_DS_CHUNK_MIN`, exp12, f16 chunks): 1.8, 1.8, 2.2, 2.4 s.
- **At 32 the loadgen's 42-46-token prompts chunk too and lose** (exp12, two sessions each: first token 0.92 /
  0.93 -> 1.22 / 1.22 s alone; three at once 13.35 / 12.55 -> 10.24 / 10.35 tok/s per request, TTFT p90 2.23 /
  2.37 -> 3.93 / 3.96 s); at 64 they stay in windows and the loadgen reads as before. 128 against 64 (exp13,
  three-turn loadgen at c3): wall 56.8 / 58.5 s, TTFT p90 3.65 / 2.09 s, and the cold 79-token prompt stays at
  3.6 s: 64.
- **A short chunk's blind prefetch reads for nothing.** 79 rows route to 137 of a layer's experts; the prefetch
  job picks before the routing. chunk117, `QX_DS_CHUNK_PREFETCH=0` against default, twice each (equal to ~5 ms):
  79 rows 1,805 -> 1,523 ms (NVMe records 1,731 -> 1,050), 128: 1,809 -> 1,680, 186: 2,145 -> 2,059, 232: 2,370
  -> 2,285; 413: 2,176 -> 2,337, 1,638: 3,406 -> 4,040, 3,293: 4,531 -> 5,741. The crossover is between 232 and
  413 rows.
- **f16 on short chunks is inside the contract by three rows** (chunk119, default against the exact knob in
  `qx-ds-check`, the start of `route16k.tokens`: an 88-row chunk and the 512 single steps after it): 513 rows, KL
  mean 0.0186, median 0.0011, p99 0.24, max 1.00 (row 98: not a near-tie), top-1 491/513 = 95.7% (bound: 488);
  rows 471-599 read 117/129 = 90.7%. A 136-row chunk and 64 steps of the same text (exp13; dumps not kept): KL
  0.0212, top-1 62/65. The adjudicator held chunk119 on this: one text, no margin. The exact kernels cost
  0.03-0.27 s on such a chunk, so short chunks keep them (below).
- **The ring size is back-pressure, not loss** (backlog item 6): `QX_DS_STAGE_CAP=512` on a 16k prompt takes the
  ring-slot waits from 961 + 944 ms to 56 + 0 and leaves the chunks at 4,779 + 7,070 / 4,784 + 7,074 ms against
  4,794 + 6,972 / 4,802 + 6,961. Closed.

### Change
- `worker_ds.rs`: `QX_DS_CHUNK_MIN` (default 64; was max(window, 256)): the shortest prompt stretch that runs in
  chunks, alone or beside decoders.
- `mod.rs`: `QX_DS_CHUNK_F16_MIN` (default 256): a chunk of fewer rows keeps the exact kernels, so the 64-255-token
  prompts stay bit-identical to the windows they ran in until now, in default mode too. (The comment at
  `chunk_min` in `worker_ds.rs` quotes exp12's f16 times, 2.2 and 2.4 s at 186 and 232 tokens; exact: 2.22, 2.52.)
- `mod.rs`: `QX_DS_CHUNK_PREFETCH_MIN` (default 256): a chunk of fewer rows posts no prefetch job (the path of
  `QX_DS_CHUNK_PREFETCH=0`); a layer without one zeroes `chunk_used_gen` (breaker: the generation counter wraps
  after 65,535 layers and a later prefetch job could otherwise skip by a stale routing).
- `driver.rs`, the race the breaker flagged on 2026-11-12: `chunk_ram` holds the records a chunk job staged from
  their RAM places since the last settle; `chunk_guard` settles before such a place changes hands (`promote_one`'s
  victim, the swap's duplicate in `adapt`). Counted in the lane line: 0 settles in a warmed 2k prompt, 1 in a 16k
  prompt, 3 in the four chunks of two prompts at once, 0 in prefill256k's two chunks and in the short chunks.
  `chunk_settle` takes `Shared::capture` (it can now run from the idle loop).
- `driver.rs`: a prompt chunk flags its tick (`Shared::chunk_tick`), so the eviction cap of 2026-11-11 applies to
  chunks of 128 rows or fewer too (they did not exist in the server before).
- A panic of the engine thread ends the process from a panic hook (`_exit(101)` before any unwinding: dropping
  the engine joins the driver thread); a panic of the driver thread does the same from a drop guard.
  `QX_DS_PANIC_AFTER_CHUNKS=N` panics the engine thread after its Nth chunk (test).

Three-slot server, `--ctx 262144 --window 128`, `QX_DS_VRAM=20 QX_DS_RAM=95`, chunk120, default mode, one run
each (windows = `QX_DS_CHUNK_MIN=100000`; prompt time + the 64-token reply):

| prompt (fresh server, in this order) | windows | chunks (default) | with the blind prefetch (`PREFETCH_MIN=0`) | in f16 (`F16_MIN=0`) |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| 79 tokens | 3.58 + 4.14 s | 1.54 + 3.91 s | 1.80 s | 1.52 s |
| 186 tokens | 8.52 + 2.82 | 2.22 + 2.96 | 2.31 | 2.06 |
| 232 tokens | 9.35 + 2.72 | 2.52 + 2.98 | 2.60 | 2.27 |
| 128 tokens | 7.02 + 2.94 | 1.78 + 2.93 | 1.89 | 1.67 |
| 111 tokens (the fifth request: tiers warmed) | 2.27 + 2.36 | 1.64 + 2.53 | 1.77 | 1.57 |
| 79 tokens, three times (warm repeats) | 3.56, 2.08, 1.55 | 1.54, 1.46, 1.45 | | |
| 128 tokens, three times | 7.13, 2.54, 2.32 | 1.78, 1.75, 1.74 | | |
| after a shared prefix (exact knob): 156 new tokens at 1,626 | 3.09 | 1.94 | | |
| 127 new tokens at 1,770 | 2.03 | 1.65 | | |

The reply after a chunk is 0.14-0.26 s slower than after windows on the 186-, 232- and 111-token rows (a chunk
leaves the tiers less adapted to the prompt than its windows did); prompt plus reply: 7.72 -> 5.45, 11.34 -> 5.18,
12.07 -> 5.50, 9.96 -> 4.71, 4.63 -> 4.17 s.

Three-turn loadgen (`--levels 1,3 --rounds 2 --turns 3 --max-tokens 64 --warmup 6`; turns 2 and 3 are stretches
of ~120-230 tokens), windows -> chunks: one client: wall 23.4 -> 22.1 s, TTFT median / p90 1.43 / 2.59 -> 1.33 /
1.87 s, per-request decode 25.94 -> 25.63 tok/s; three clients: wall 61.3 -> 55.9 s, aggregate 18.78 -> 20.60
tok/s, TTFT 2.95 / 5.77 -> 1.45 / 2.03 s, per-request decode 9.31 -> 8.43 tok/s (-9%: a decoder waits behind the
others' chunks; chunk119 read 9.00 -> 8.32, chunk117 9.33 -> 8.08, exp13 9.19 -> 8.08, those three with f16
short chunks). The trade: the first token comes in half the time and the session ends 9% sooner; each stream
decodes 8-13% slower under three clients.

Long prompts: three sequential prompts 10.97 / 4.51 / 3.41 s (default; as before); two at once and A beside B
were rerun with the exact knob only (31.49 / 30.13 s; 18.78 s and A's reply 24.37 s: as chunk115).

### Breaker (fresh context, three passes: chunk116's, chunk118's and chunk120's diffs; code reading, no runs)
No defect that corrupts results. Its notes, handled: `chunk_settle` synced events without the capture lock
(taken now); a short chunk beside a decoder and a rewind followed by a short chunk were unrun paths (run:
above); the default of 64 was shown only on cold, solitary prompts (warm repeats and the three-turn loadgen:
above, with the per-request decode cost); the reply after a chunk is slower (in the table); the eviction cap
classed chunks of 128 rows or fewer as window ticks (`chunk_tick`; no measurement shows a change: the reply after
the 79-token chunk 3.91 against 4.44 s once, the others equal); a drop guard would hang in the engine's drop when
the driver is the cause (panic hook); the generation wrap (zeroed); a short exact chunk before the first f16
chunk (chain X1). Confirmed by reading: no other path gives a RAM place away while a chunk copy may read it;
`chunk_ffn_wide` is the only f16 switch (`blas_map` is filled under it and cleared at each chunk's end); the
hook acts only on the thread named `qx-ds-engine` and `start` has one call site; set D's prompts make no chunk;
the 8k server has no chunk path (`QX_DS_CHUNK` is set only from 32k of context).

### Adjudication (fresh context)
HOLD on chunk119: the speed claims, the guard, the panic exit, the gate arithmetic, ds-checks, like-for-like
and every recomputed figure held; what did not was making f16 the default for 64-255-token prompts on the
evidence above (one text, the top-1 bound cleared by three rows, a tail at 90.7%). Answered by not doing it:
short chunks keep the exact kernels (`CHUNK_F16_MIN`), which the adjudicator had noted costs 0.03-0.27 s.
HOLD on chunk120 for two missing checks (a second identity session on the binary; a short exact chunk after an
f16 chunk in default mode), LANDED once both read as specified: chain V26, above. Not a promotion: no
scoreboard row, the baseline stays 19.166. Verified on chunk120: the gate (no flagged row), ds-checks, the
crates diff equal to the run directory's, every table and trace figure recomputed, `ffn wide` false on the
short chunks and true on the long ones.

- `QX_DS_CHUNK_MIN=32` and 128 (above).
- f16 for chunks under 256 rows (above): 1.52 / 2.06 / 2.27 / 1.67 / 1.57 s against 1.54 / 2.22 / 2.52 / 1.78 /
  1.64 exact; behind `QX_DS_CHUNK_F16_MIN=0`.
- V21's KL run (200 tokens with a 512-token tail) made no chunk and compared two identical runs: void.


## 2026-11-14: A prompt chunk's prefetch follows the next layer's predicted routing, and the picks predicted without rows wait for the routing — LANDED (three-slot 256k server, fresh prompts: 298 tokens 2.46 -> 2.07 s, 413: 2.31 -> 2.06, 582: 2.61 -> 2.39, 872: 2.71 -> 2.50, 960: 2.94 -> 2.76, 1,638: 3.17 -> 3.14; replies byte-identical; set D 19.167 against 19.166); run `perf/results/2026-11-14/setd-promo28-013028` (adjudication below)

Tree: a3f0f10 plus `driver.rs`, `mod.rs` (`git diff -- crates` sha 92cdd44d6a0c), committed with this entry;
binaries `~/qx-bins/chunk122` (qx-ds-check d0f057bcff3d, quixiinfer db73acff0661, qx-ds-multi 132279853400,
qx-ds-replay 5b4a42be0f40), in `target/release`. chunk121 was the build the breaker read; chunk122 adds its
hardening note and the cap for the exact kernels. Chains: PR1-PR14 (screening, worktree builds), W1-W3
(chunk121/122, timings and server arms), V27 (the validation recipe), W5 (the gate run).

### What was measured first
- **The next layer's router on this layer's FFN input names most of the experts the chunk will use.** Layer
  l + 1's router (norm, router GEMV, top 12) on layer l's `xpre`, for every row, read back with layer l's
  selections; the union over the rows against the experts layer l + 1 then routes to, NVMe-tier experts only:
  2,040 rows: top-8 recall 83.9%, precision 79.7%; top-12 96.9% / 70.0%. 256 rows: top-8 63.2% / 51.9%, top-12
  87.0% / 34.8%. The later guess (the layer's own router on the residual before its attention) is sharper (2,040
  rows 91.0% / 83.3%, 256 rows 78.0% / 64.8%) but needs a sync per layer and comes after the experts: not built.
- **The hit rate rises with the predicted row count and hardly depends on the chunk's size.** Score = rows that
  have the expert in their predicted top 8, plus a quarter per row that has it in ranks 9-12. Used / experts
  outside VRAM by score class, 256 | 592 | 2,040 rows: score 0: 8% | 11% | 20%; under 1: 24% | 27% | 37%; 1: 48%
  | 50% | 52%; 2: 66% | 64% | 69%; 3: 72% | 71% | 77%; 4-5: 81% | 79% | 80%; 6-7: 90% | 85% | 89%; 8 and more:
  91-96% | 91-98% | 91-99%.
- **A chunk's time follows the records it stages, blind ones included.** Warmed single chunk in `qx-ds-check`,
  old order: 256 rows 1.84 s with 7,562 records staged for 5,644 used; 2,040 rows 3.24 s with 12,382 for 10,805.
  Before the routing arrives the old prefetch job had submitted 1,224 NVMe reads at 256 rows (365 of them used)
  and 2,392 at 2,040 rows (1,777 used).
- **The ceiling of any predictor** (the same chunk's own routing replayed from a file as the prediction): 256 rows
  1.43 s, 592 rows 1.86 s, 2,040 rows 2.97 s.

### Change
- `mod.rs`, `forward_chunk_inner`: in each layer's slab loop `chunk_predict` runs layer l + 1's router on the
  slab's FFN input into a small device buffer (rows x 12 ids, allocated per chunk outside the flex tail); after
  the layer's sync the ids come back with the selections and become `pred`: predicted rows per expert. Layer
  l + 1's prefetch job carries `pred` and `pred_t`. Only where a chunk prefetches (256 rows or more) and up to
  `QX_DS_CHUNK_PRED_MAX` rows (2,048; 768 with the exact kernels of `QX_DS_PREFILL_EXACT=1`).
- `driver.rs`, `serve_chunk`: the pick is ordered by tier, then predicted rows, then score, and split at
  `pred_t` (`QX_DS_CHUNK_PRED_T`, default 3). The picks under it are held back: no read of theirs is submitted
  and no record staged until the engine has published the layer's routing; then the existing skip drops the
  unused ones. The loop leaves the hold when the published generation is this job's, or is neither this job's
  nor the one at the job's start (the engine went past: the rest is staged blind, as before), or after 5 s.
- The prediction decides the order and timing of staging only. With no prediction (layer 0, chunks under 256 or
  over 2,048 rows, `QX_DS_CHUNK_PRED=0`) the job is the old one.
- Chunk log: NVMe reads submitted before the routing, the used ones among them, the time held;
  `QX_DS_CHUNK_PRED=2` adds the predictor's accuracy and the score classes.

### Validation (chunk122)
- Tail dumps of 2k and 16k prompts byte-identical to chunk113's. Exact kernels, 592-row chunk, predictor on

- Server: replies to the 13,283 / 3,293 / 1,638-token prompts the same bytes as `v12b-old`'s; the six W3 prompts
  the same bytes on against off, in default and in exact mode, and chunk122's the same as chunk121's.
- Exact, two long prompts at once: 60/60 and 60/60 row hashes equal to `t0`. A beside B: 98/98 against `t9a`,
  64/64 against `t9b`. A 79-token chunk beside a decoder: 98/98 and 60/60. Short prompts by chunks against
  windows: 316/316 decode rows in exact and in default mode, texts the same. Rewind: 190 common rows equal (127
  from the start, 63 from the end), texts the same.
- A predicted chunk beside a decoder, then two predicted chunks at once (exact knob, W3b): the four replies the
  same bytes on against off; the 582-token prompt arriving a second into a reply 3.03 -> 2.89 s, 298 tokens
  2.60 -> 2.19, the 960-token one 3.71 (cap), 713 tokens beside it 3.48 -> 3.34.

  The user's Windows VM ran from 00:38:01 to 01:03:34 (journal), starting three minutes before that run
